\displaystyle{\bf E}\big[\mathtt{U}(\mathbf{T}_{0})^{2}\big]=\frac{\mathtt{m}_{\nu}(2)\!-\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}}{\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{2}\!-\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}},

{\bf E}\big[\Phi_{2}(\mathbf{T},\varrho)\big]\!=\!\tfrac{1}{2\mathtt{m}_{\nu}}{\bf E}\Big[\mathtt{m}_{\nu}\mathtt{U}(\mathbf{T}_{0})^{2}+\!\sum_{u\in\mathbf{T}_{0}}\!\mathtt{U}(\theta_{u}\mathbf{T}_{0})^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{\overleftarrow{u}=\varnothing\}}\Big]\!=\!{\bf E}\big[\mathtt{U}(\mathbf{T}_{0})^{2}\big]\!=\!\frac{\mathtt{m}_{\nu}(2)\!-\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}}{\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{2}\!-\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}},

the last equality being a consequence of the explicit computation of the variance of GW processes: see e.g. Athreya & Ney
[
4
, Chapter I, (2) p. 4]
.
\hfill\blacksquare

We next provides comparison bounds of harmonic coordinates and relative heights of vertices.

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
\nu
be a supercritical offspring distribution whose mean is denoted by
\mathtt{m}_{\nu}
.
We assume that
\mathtt{m}_{\nu}(2)\!=\!\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}k^{2}\nu(k)\!<\!\infty
.
Let
(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})
be an infinite tree GW(
\nu
)-tree as in Definition
2.16

or an invariant GW(
\nu
)-tree as in Definition
2.21
. Let the r.v.s
U_{x},S_{x}
,
x\!\in\!\mathbf{T}
, be as in (
3.2
) and (
3.3
). Recall from (
2.15
) the notation
\partial L_{\mathtt{o}}
and
\mathtt{o}(p)
,
p\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
.
Then the following holds true.

(i)

For all
p\!\in\!\mathbb{N}^{*}
we set
\bm{\beta}_{p}\!=\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-1}\sum_{x\in\partial L_{\mathtt{o}}:\overleftarrow{x}=\mathtt{o}(p)}U_{x}
.
Then
\bm{\beta}_{p}\!=\!U_{\mathtt{o}(p)}\!-\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-1}U_{\mathtt{o}(p-1)}
. Moreover, the r.v.s
(\bm{\beta}_{p})_{p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
are
i.i.d. such that
{\bf E}[\bm{\beta}_{1}]\!=\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-2}(\mathtt{m}_{\nu}(2)\!-\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu})
. If
\mathtt{b}\!\in\!(1,\infty)
is such that

\mathtt{m}_{\nu}(\mathtt{b}+1)\!:=\!\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}k^{\mathtt{b}+1}\nu(k)\!<\!\infty
, then
{\bf E}[\bm{\beta}_{1}^{\mathtt{b}}]\!<\!\infty
.

(ii)

Recall
\sigma_{\nu}
from (
3.6
) and assume that there exists
\mathtt{b}\!\in\!(1,\infty)
such that

\mathtt{m}_{\nu}(2\mathtt{b}+1)\!=\!

\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}

k^{2\mathtt{b}+1}\nu(k)

\!<\!\infty
. Then, there is a constant
c\!\in\!(0,\infty)
that only depends on
\nu
and
\mathtt{b}
such that for all
p\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
,

{\bf E}\big[\big|\tfrac{1}{\sigma^{2}_{\nu}}S_{\mathtt{o}(p)}\!-\!\bm{|}\!\!|\mathtt{o}(p)\bm{|}\!\!|\big|^{2\mathtt{b}}\big]\!\leq\!c\,p^{\mathtt{b}}
.
Moreover, for all
\beta\!\in\!(\tfrac{1}{2}+\tfrac{1}{2\mathtt{b}},1)
, we set

R_{\beta}=\sup_{p\in\mathbb{N}}\,\frac{\big|\tfrac{1}{\sigma^{2}_{\nu}}S_{\mathtt{o}(p)}\!-\!\bm{|}\!\!|\mathtt{o}(p)\bm{|}\!\!|\big|}{1+p^{\beta}}.

(3.7)

Then, there exists a constant
c^{\prime}\!\in\!(0,\infty)
that only depends on
\nu
,
\mathtt{b}
and
\beta
such that for all
\lambda\!\in\!(0,\infty)
, we get
{\bf P}\big(R_{\beta}\geq\lambda\big)\!\leq\!c^{\prime}\lambda^{-2\mathtt{b}}
.

Proof.

The first point of
(i)
is a direct consequence of the definition of invariant GW trees and of (
3.2
).
Then, observe that
{\bf E}[\bm{\beta}_{1}|k_{\mathtt{o}(1)}(\mathbf{T})]\!=\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-1}{\bf E}[\mathtt{U}(\mathbf{T}^{\prime})](k_{\mathtt{o}(1)}(\mathbf{T})\!-\!1)\!=\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-1}(k_{\mathtt{o}(1)}(\mathbf{T})\!-\!1)
, where
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
stands for a GW(
\nu
)-tree. Thus,
{\bf E}[\bm{\beta}_{1}]\!=\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-2}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}k(k\!-\!1)\nu(k)\!=\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-2}(\mathtt{m}_{\nu}(2)\!-\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu})
.
More generally, note that

\bm{\beta}_{1}^{\mathtt{b}}\!\leq\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-\mathtt{b}}(k_{\mathtt{o}(1)}(\mathbf{T})\!-\!1)^{\mathtt{b}-1}\sum_{x\in\partial L_{\mathtt{o}}:\overleftarrow{x}=\mathtt{o}(1)}U^{\mathtt{b}}_{x}
since
z\mapsto z^{\mathtt{b}}
is convex. Then recall from Lemma
3.1

(ii)
that
{\bf E}[\mathtt{U}^{\mathtt{b}}(\mathbf{T}^{\prime})]\!<\!\infty
if
\mathtt{m}_{\nu}(\mathtt{b})\!<\!\infty
.
Thus
{\bf E}[\bm{\beta}_{1}^{\mathtt{b}}|k_{\mathtt{o}(1)}(\mathbf{T})]\!\leq\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-\mathtt{b}}{\bf E}[\mathtt{U}^{\mathtt{b}}(\mathbf{T}^{\prime})](k_{\mathtt{o}(1)}(\mathbf{T})\!-\!1)^{\mathtt{b}}
, which implies that
{\bf E}[\bm{\beta}_{1}^{\mathtt{b}}]\!\leq\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-\mathtt{b}-1}{\bf E}[\mathtt{U}^{\mathtt{b}}(\mathbf{T}^{\prime})]\mathtt{m}_{\nu}(\mathtt{b}+1)\!<\!\infty
.

Let us prove
(ii)
. As an immediate consequence of
(i)
, for all
p\!\in\!\mathbb{N}^{*}
we get

U_{\mathtt{o}(p)}=\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-p}U_{\mathtt{o}}+\!\!\sum_{1\leq k\leq p}\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-(p-k)}\bm{\beta}_{k}\;=\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-p}U_{\mathtt{o}}+(1\!-\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-1})^{-1}{\bf E}\big[\bm{\beta}_{p-\mathtt{G}}{\bf 1}_{\{0\leq\mathtt{G}<p\}}\big|(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})\big]

(3.8)

where
\mathtt{G}
stands for a r.v. that is independent from
(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})
and such that
{\bf P}(\mathtt{G}\!=\!k)\!=\!(1\!-\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-1})\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-k}
,
k\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
. Since
S_{\mathtt{o}(p-1)}\!-\!S_{\mathtt{o}(p)}\!=\!U_{\mathtt{o}(p-1)}
(as a consequence of (
3.3
)), we get by (
3.8
)

\displaystyle-S_{\mathtt{o}(p)}\!\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!U_{\mathtt{o}}+\!\!\sum_{1\leq q<p}U_{\mathtt{o}(q)}\,=\,\frac{1\!-\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-p}}{1\!-\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-1}}U_{\mathtt{o}}\,+\!\!\sum_{1\leq k<p}\!\!\bm{\beta}_{k}\!\!\sum_{k\leq q<p}\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-(q-k)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\frac{1\!-\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-p}}{1\!-\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-1}}U_{\mathtt{o}}\,+\!\!\sum_{1\leq k<p}\!\!\bm{\beta}_{k}\frac{1\!-\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-(p-k)}}{1\!-\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-1}}\,=\frac{1\!-\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-p}}{1\!-\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-1}}U_{\mathtt{o}}+\!\!\sum_{1\leq k<p}\!\!\frac{\bm{\beta}_{k}}{1\!-\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-1}}-\!\!\sum_{1\leq k<p}\!\!\frac{\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-(p-k)}\bm{\beta}_{k}}{1\!-\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-1}}.

To simplify notation, we set
a\!=\!{\bf E}[\bm{\beta}_{1}]
and
\overline{\bm{\beta}}_{k}\!=\!\bm{\beta}_{k}-a
, that is a centered r.v. and we get

\displaystyle-(1\!-\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-1})S_{\mathtt{o}(p)}-ap\!\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\Sigma_{p-1}+R_{p}(1)-R_{p}(2)\quad\textrm{where}

\displaystyle\Sigma_{p-1}\!\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\sum_{1\leq k\leq p-1}\!\!\overline{\bm{\beta}}_{k}\,,\qquad R_{p}(1)=(1\!-\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-p})U_{\mathtt{o}}\!-\!a\quad\textrm{and}

\displaystyle R_{p}(2)\!\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\sum_{1\leq k<p}\!\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-(p-k)}\bm{\beta}_{k}=(1\!-\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-1})^{-1}{\bf E}\big[\bm{\beta}_{p-\mathtt{G}}{\bf 1}_{\{0<\mathtt{G}<p\}}\big|(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})\big].

Note from
(i)
and (
3.6
) that
a^{-1}(1\!-\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-1})\!=\!\sigma_{\nu}^{-2}
. Since
z\mapsto z^{2\mathtt{b}}
is convex, we get

a^{2\mathtt{b}}{\bf E}\Big[\big|\tfrac{1}{\sigma^{2}_{\nu}}S_{\mathtt{o}(p)}\!+\!p\,\big|^{2\mathtt{b}}\Big]\leq 3^{2\mathtt{b}-1}\big({\bf E}\big[|R_{p}(1)|^{2\mathtt{b}}\big]+{\bf E}\big[|R_{p}(2)|^{2\mathtt{b}}\big]+{\bf E}\big[|\Sigma_{p-1}|^{2\mathtt{b}}\big]\big)\;.

First observe that
{\bf E}[|R_{p}(1)|^{2\mathtt{b}}]\leq 2^{2\mathtt{b}-1}({\bf E}[U_{\mathtt{o}}^{2\mathtt{b}}]+a^{2\mathtt{b}})
, which finite by Lemma
3.1

(ii)
.

To bound
{\bf E}\big[|R_{p}(2)|^{2\mathtt{b}}\big]
, we use conditional Jensen inequality to get

|R_{p}(2)|^{2\mathtt{b}}\!\leq\!(1\!-\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-1})^{-2\mathtt{b}}{\bf E}\big[\bm{\beta}^{2\mathtt{b}}_{p-\mathtt{G}}{\bf 1}_{\{0<\mathtt{G}<p\}}\big|(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})\big]\;,

which entails
{\bf E}\big[|R_{p}(2)|^{2\mathtt{b}}\big]\!\leq\!(1\!-\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-1})^{-2\mathtt{b}}{\bf E}\big[\bm{\beta}^{2\mathtt{b}}_{1}\big]
, which is finite by
(i)
.

Let us bound
{\bf E}\big[|\Sigma_{p-1}|^{2\mathtt{b}}\big]
.
Since the
\overline{\bm{\beta}}_{k}
are i.i.d. centered and such that
{\bf E}\big[|\overline{\bm{\beta}}_{k}|^{2\mathtt{b}}]\!<\!\infty
by
(i)
, we can apply Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality (see see e.g. Burkholder
[
13
, Theorem 9 p. 1502]
) to get a constant
c_{0}\!\in\!(0,\infty)
that only depends on
\mathtt{b}
such that

{\bf E}\big[|\Sigma_{p-1}|^{2\mathtt{b}}\big]\leq c_{0}\,{\bf E}\Big[\Big(\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{\hskip 8.19447pt1\leq k<p}\!\!\!\!\!\overline{\bm{\beta}}_{k}^{2}\Big)^{\mathtt{b}}\Big].

Since
z\mapsto z^{\mathtt{b}}
is convex,

{\bf E}\Big[\Big(\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{\hskip 8.19447pt1\leq k<p}\!\!\!\!\!\overline{\bm{\beta}}_{k}^{2}\Big)^{\mathtt{b}}\Big]\leq(p\!-\!1)^{\mathtt{b}-1}\!\!\!\sum_{1\leq k<p}\!\!\!{\bf E}\big[\,|\overline{\bm{\beta}}_{k}|^{2\mathtt{b}}\big]=(p\!-\!1)^{\mathtt{b}}p{\bf E}\big[\,|\overline{\bm{\beta}}_{1}|^{2\mathtt{b}}\big],

which proves the first inequality in
(ii)
. To complete the proof of
(ii)
we simply observe that

{\bf P}\big(R_{\beta}\geq\lambda\big)\!\leq\!\sum_{p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}{\bf P}\Big(\big|\tfrac{1}{\sigma^{2}_{\nu}}S_{\mathtt{o}(p)}\!-\!\bm{|}\!\!|\mathtt{o}(p)\bm{|}\!\!|\,\big|\!\geq\!\lambda(1+p^{\beta})\Big)\leq\sum_{p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}\frac{cp^{\mathtt{b}}}{\lambda^{2\mathtt{b}}(1+p^{\beta})^{2\mathtt{b}}}<c^{\prime}\lambda^{-2\mathtt{b}},

by a Markov inequality and since
(2\beta-\!1)\mathtt{b}\!>\!1
.
\hfill\blacksquare

The following lemma shows that BRWs do not visit ‘bad’ vertices, namely vertices
x

such that
\sigma_{\nu}^{-2}\times
(the harmonic coordinate at
x
) is large compared to the relative height
\bm{|}\!\!|x\bm{|}\!\!|
of
x
.

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
\tau
be a GW(
\mu
)-tree, where
\mu
is a non-trivial critical offspring distribution.
Let
\nu
be a supercritical offspring distribution whose mean is denoted by
\mathtt{m}_{\nu}
.
Let
\mathtt{b}\!\in\!(1,\infty)
. We assume that
\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}k^{2\mathtt{b}+1}\nu(k)\!<\!\infty
. Let
(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})
be an invariant
GW(
\nu
)-tree. Let
(Y^{\prime}_{u})_{u\in\tau}
be a
\tau
-indexed,
\mathbf{T}
-valued
\mathtt{m}_{\nu}
-biased BRW whose conditional law given
(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})
and
\tau
is
Q^{{}_{(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})}}_{{}^{(\tau,\varnothing)}}

as in Definition
2.25
.
We recall from (
2.15
)
the pieces of notation
\mathtt{o}(p)
and
L_{\mathtt{o}}
. Let

(S_{x})_{x\in\mathbf{T}}
be the harmonic coordinates as in (
3.3
) and we recall from (
3.6
) the notation
\sigma_{\nu}
. For all
q\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
, all
\lambda\!\in\!(0,\infty)
and all
\beta\!\in\!(\tfrac{1}{2}+\tfrac{1}{2\mathtt{b}},1)
, we define the following subset of
\mathbf{T}

B_{q,\lambda,\beta}=\Big\{x\!\in\!\mathbf{T}\backslash L_{\mathtt{o}}\,:\bm{|}\!\!|\mathtt{o}\wedge x\bm{|}\!\!|\!\geq\!-q\;\,\textrm{and}\;\,\big|\frac{{}_{{}_{1}}}{{}^{{}^{\sigma^{2}_{\nu}}}}(S_{x}\!-\!S_{\mathtt{o}\wedge x})\!-\!\big(\bm{|}\!\!|x\bm{|}\!\!|\!-\!\bm{|}\!\!|\mathtt{o}\wedge x\bm{|}\!\!|\big)\big|\!\geq\!\lambda\big(1+\big(\bm{|}\!\!|x\bm{|}\!\!|\!-\!\bm{|}\!\!|\mathtt{o}\wedge x\bm{|}\!\!|\big)^{\beta}\big)\Big\}.

Then, there exists
c\!\in\!(0,\infty)
, which only depends on
\nu,\mathtt{b},\beta
, such that

{\bf P}\big(\exists u\!\in\!\tau:Y^{\prime}_{u}\!\in\!B_{q,\lambda,\beta})\!\leq\!cq\lambda^{-2\mathtt{b}}.

Proof.
Let
(\mathbf{T}_{0},\varnothing)
be a GW(
\nu
)-tree (Definition
2.6

(\textbf{a})
). Denote by
(S^{0}_{x})_{x\in\mathbf{T}_{0}}
its harmonic coordinates. We first bound the expectation of

Z(\mathbf{T}_{0})=\sum_{x\in\mathbf{T}_{0}}\tfrac{\mathtt{m}_{\nu}+k_{x}(\mathbf{T}_{0})}{\mathtt{m}_{\nu}-1}\,\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-|x|}{\bf 1}_{\!\big\{\big|\sigma_{\nu}^{-2}S^{0}_{x}-|x|\big|\geq\lambda(1+|x|^{\beta})\big\}}.

Let
(\mathbf{T}^{\prime},\mathtt{o}^{\prime})
be an infinite GW(
\nu
)-tree (Definition
2.16
) whose harmonic coordinates are denoted by

(S^{\prime}_{x})_{x\in\mathbf{T}^{\prime}}
. We recall that
S^{0}_{\varnothing}\!=\!0
and that for all
x\!\in\!\mathbf{T}_{0}
,
S^{0}_{x}\!=\!\sum_{y\in\,]\!]\varnothing,x]\!]}\mathtt{U}(\theta_{y}\mathbf{T}_{0})
.
Then, the many-to-one formula (
2.22
) implies

\displaystyle{\bf E}\big[Z(\mathbf{T}_{0})\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}{\bf E}\Big[\tfrac{\mathtt{m}_{\nu}+k_{\mathtt{o}}(\mathbf{T}^{\prime})}{\mathtt{m}_{\nu}-1}\,{\bf 1}_{\!\big\{\big|\sigma_{\nu}^{-2}S^{\prime}_{\mathtt{o}^{\prime}(p)}-\bm{|}\!\!|\mathtt{o}^{\prime}(p)\bm{|}\!\!|\big|\geq\lambda(1+|p|^{\beta})\big\}}\Big]

(3.9)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\tfrac{2\mathtt{m}_{\nu}}{\mathtt{m}_{\nu}-1}\!\!\sum_{p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}{\bf P}\big(\big|\sigma_{\nu}^{-2}S_{\mathtt{o}(p)}-\bm{|}\!\!|\mathtt{o}(p)\bm{|}\!\!|\big|\geq\lambda(1+|p|^{\beta})\big)\leq\sum_{p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}\frac{cp^{\mathtt{b}}}{\lambda^{2\mathtt{b}}(1+p^{\beta})^{2\mathtt{b}}}<c^{\prime}\lambda^{-2\mathtt{b}},

by the absolute continuity relation (
2.33
) between the laws of
\mathbf{T}
and
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
, by Lemma
3.2

(ii)
and since
2\beta(\mathtt{b}\!-\!1)\!>\!1
.

We now complete the proof of the lemma. To that end, we denote by
\overline{X}_{n}\!=\!(\mathbf{T},X_{n})
,
n\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
, a
\mathtt{m}_{\nu}
-biased RW on
\mathbf{T}
starting at
X_{0}\!=\!\mathtt{o}
. By definition of BRW, we first get

{\bf P}\big(\exists u\!\in\!\tau:Y^{\prime}_{u}\!\in\!B_{q,\lambda,\beta}\big|\,(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o}),\tau\big)\leq\sum_{u\in\tau}{\bf E}\big[{\bf 1}_{\{Y^{\prime}_{u}\in B_{q,\lambda,\beta}\}}\big|\,(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o}),\tau\big]=\sum_{u\in\tau}P_{\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o}}(X_{|u|}\!\in\!B_{q,\lambda,\beta}\big)\;.

The many-to-one formula (
2.22
) is applied to
\tau
whose mean number of children is one and we get

\displaystyle{\bf E}\big[\sum_{u\in\tau}P_{\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o}}(X_{|u|}\!\in\!B_{q,\lambda,\beta}\big)\big|(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}P_{\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o}}(X_{n}\!\in\!B_{q,\lambda,\beta}\big)=\sum_{x\in B_{q,\lambda,\beta}}G(\mathtt{o},x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\mathtt{m}_{\nu}}\sum_{y\in\partial L_{\mathtt{o}}}{\bf 1}_{\{\bm{|}\!\!|\mathtt{o}\wedge y\bm{|}\!\!|\geq-q\}}Z(\theta_{y}\mathbf{T}).

Here, we used the fact that the Green function
G(\mathtt{o},x)
is equal to
\tfrac{\mathtt{m}_{\nu}+k_{x}(\mathbf{T})}{\mathtt{m}_{\nu}-1}\,\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-(\bm{|}\!\!|x\bm{|}\!\!|-\bm{|}\!\!|\mathtt{o}\wedge x\bm{|}\!\!|)}
by (
2.32
). Recall from the definition of invariant and infinite GW trees that conditionally on
\partial L_{\mathtt{o}}
, the trees
(\theta_{y}\mathbf{T})_{y\in\partial L_{\mathtt{o}}}
are i.i.d. GW(
\nu
)-trees. Therefore,

{\bf P}\big(\exists u\!\in\!\tau:Y^{\prime}_{u}\!\in\!B_{q,\lambda,\beta}\big|\,\partial L_{\mathtt{o}}\big)\leq\tfrac{1}{\mathtt{m}_{\nu}}\sum_{y\in\partial L_{\mathtt{o}}}{\bf 1}_{\{-\bm{|}\!\!|\mathtt{o}\wedge y\bm{|}\!\!|\leq q\}}{\bf E}[Z(\mathbf{T}_{0})]\,\leq\,\tfrac{1}{\mathtt{m}_{\nu}}{\bf E}[Z(\mathbf{T}_{0})]\sum_{0\leq j\leq q}k_{\mathtt{o}(j)}(\mathbf{T})

which implies the desired result by (
3.9
) and since
{\bf E}[k_{\mathtt{o}(j)}(\mathbf{T})]\!=\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{-1}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}k^{2}\nu(k)\!<\!\infty
if
j\!\geq\!1
and
{\bf E}[k_{\mathtt{o}}(\mathbf{T})]\!=\!(2\mathtt{m}_{\nu})^{-1}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}k(k+\mathtt{m}_{\nu})\nu(k)\!<\!\infty
.
\hfill\blacksquare

We next get upper bound of the (quenched moments of the) increments of the martingale
(S_{X_{n}})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
.

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
\nu
be a supercritical offspring distribution whose mean is denoted by
\mathtt{m}_{\nu}
.
Let
\mathtt{b}_{0}\!\in\!(1,\infty)
. We assume that
\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}k^{2\mathtt{b}_{0}+1}\nu(k)\!<\!\infty
. Let
(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})
be an invariant GW(
\nu
)-tree as in Definition
2.21
.
Let the r.v.s
U_{x}
,
x\!\in\!\mathbf{T}
, be as in (
3.2
). Let
\overline{X}_{n}\!=\!(\mathbf{T},X_{n})
,
n\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
, be a
\mathtt{m}_{\nu}
-biased RW on
\mathbf{T}
such that
X_{0}\!=\!\mathtt{o}
, and recall that
P_{(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})}
stands for the conditional law of
(X_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
given
(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})
.
Then, for all
\mathtt{b}\!\in\!(1,\mathtt{b}_{0})
, there exists a
{\bf P}
-a.s. finite r.v.
K_{\mathtt{b}}(\mathbf{T})
that is a measurable function of
\mathbf{T}
and
\mathtt{b}
such that for all
m\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
and all
n\!\in\!\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

\textrm{${\bf P}$-a.s.\penalty\ }\qquad E_{\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o}}\big[|S_{X_{m+n}}\!\!-\!S_{X_{m}}|^{2\mathtt{b}}\big]\leq K_{\mathtt{b}}(\mathbf{T})\,n^{\mathtt{b}-1}(m+n).

(3.10)

Proof.
Recall the notation
\Delta_{k}\!=\!S_{X_{k+1}}\!\!-\!S_{X_{k}}
from Lemma
3.1

(iv)
. Since the
\Delta_{k}
are increments of martingales, the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality
(see e.g. Burkholder
[
13
, Theorem 9 p. 1502]
) implies that there is a constant
c(\mathtt{b})\!\in\!(0,\infty)
that only depends on
\mathtt{b}
such that

E_{\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o}}\Big[\max_{1\leq k\leq n}\big|S_{X_{m+k}}\!\!-\!S_{X_{m}}\big|^{2\mathtt{b}}\Big]\leq c(\mathtt{b})\,E_{\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o}}\Big[\Big(\!\!\!\sum_{\;\,0\leq k<n}\!\!\!\Delta_{m+k}^{2}\Big)^{\!\mathtt{b}}\Big].

Since
b\!\in\!(1,\infty)
,
z\mapsto z^{b}
is convex and we get

(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\;0\leq k<n}\Delta_{m+k}^{2})^{\mathtt{b}}\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\;0\leq k<n}|\Delta_{m+k}|^{2\mathtt{b}}
.
Then by (
3.5
) in Lemma
3.1
, we get

\displaystyle E_{\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o}}\Big[\max_{1\leq k\leq n}\big|S_{X_{m+k}}\!\!-\!S_{X_{m}}\big|^{2\mathtt{b}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq c(\mathtt{b})n^{\mathtt{b}-1}\!\!\sum_{\;\,0\leq k<n}E_{\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o}}\big[|\Delta_{m+k}|^{2\mathtt{b}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq c(\mathtt{b})n^{\mathtt{b}-1}\!\!\sum_{\;\,0\leq k<n}E_{\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o}}\Big[\tfrac{\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{2\mathtt{b}}+\mathtt{m}_{\nu}}{\mathtt{m}_{\nu}+k_{X_{m+k}}\!(\mathbf{T})}U^{2\mathtt{b}}_{X_{k+m}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq c(\mathtt{b})(\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{2\mathtt{b}}\!+\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu})\,n^{\mathtt{b}-1}(m+n)\,\Big(\tfrac{1}{m+n}\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{\hskip 8.19447pt0\leq k<m+n}\!\!\!\!\!\!E_{\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o}}\big[F(\mathbf{T},X_{k})\big]\Big)

where we have set
F(T,x)\!=\!\mathtt{U}(\theta_{x}T)^{2\mathtt{b}}/(\mathtt{m}_{\nu}+k_{x}(T))
, for all
(T,x)\!\in\!\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\bullet}\!
and where we recall the notation
\mathtt{U}(\cdot)
from (
3.1
). We observe that
F(T,x)\!=\!F(\mathtt{cent}(T,x))
. Then, we set

\mathtt{b}_{1}\!=\!\mathtt{b}_{0}/\mathtt{b}\!\in\!(1,\infty)
and we observe that
{\bf E}[F(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})^{\mathtt{b}_{1}}]\leq{\bf E}[U_{\mathtt{o}}^{2\mathtt{b}_{0}}]\!<\!\infty
since

\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}k^{2\mathtt{b}_{0}+1}\nu(k)\!<\!\infty
by Lemma
3.1

(ii)
. Therefore Corollary
2.23
applies
and asserts that

\textrm{${\bf P}$-a.s.}\quad K_{\mathtt{b}}(\mathbf{T}):=c(\mathtt{b})\,(\mathtt{m}_{\nu}^{2\mathtt{b}}\!+\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu})\,\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}\,\frac{1}{n}\!\sum_{0\leq k<n}\!\!E_{\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o}}\big[F(\mathbf{T},X_{k})\big]\;<\infty,

which entails the desired result.
\hfill\blacksquare

We next use a specific form of a conditional CLT for martingales that is used to prove the finite-dimensional convergence of the harmonic coordinates of the BRW in Section
3.4
.

Proposition 3.5
.

For all
n\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
, let
(\mathcal{F}_{n,k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
be a filtration on
(\Omega,\mathscr{F})
and let
(D_{n,k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
be a sequence of real valued r.v.s. Let
(s_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of integers and let
s\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
. We make the following assumptions.

(a)

For all
n,k
,
D_{n,k}
is
\mathcal{F}_{n,k}
-measurable,
{\bf E}[D_{n,k}^{2}]\!<\!\infty
and
{\bf P}
-a.s.
{\bf E}[D_{n,k}|\mathcal{F}_{n,k-1}]\!=\!0
.

(b)

There exists a filtration
(\mathcal{G}_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
on
(\Omega,\mathscr{F})
and a sequence of integers
(r_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that

\mathcal{F}_{n,0}\!=\!\mathcal{G}_{r_{n}}
,
n\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
.

(c)

For all
\varepsilon\!\in\!(0,1)
,
{\bf P}
-a.s.
\lim_{n\to\infty}\sum_{1\leq k\leq s_{n}}{\bf E}[D_{n,k}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|D_{n,k}|>\varepsilon\}}|\mathcal{F}_{n,k-1}]=0
.

(d)

{\bf P}
-a.s.
\lim_{n\to\infty}\sum_{1\leq k\leq s_{n}}{\bf E}[D_{n,k}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{n,k-1}]\!=\!s
.

Then, we set
M_{n}\!=\!\sum_{1\leq k\leq s_{n}}D_{n,k}
and for all
\lambda\!\in\!\mathbb{R}
, we get

\textrm{${\bf P}$-a.s.\penalty\ }\quad\lim_{n\to\infty}{\bf E}\big[e^{i\lambda M_{n}}\big|\mathcal{F}_{n,0}\big]=e^{-\frac{{}_{1}}{{}^{2}}s\lambda^{2}}\;.

(3.11)

Proof.
See Appendix
C
.
\hfill\blacksquare

Remark 3.6
.

In Assumption
(b)
,
\sigma
-fields are only partially nested
(i.e.,
\mathcal{F}_{n,k}
is not necessarily included in

\mathcal{F}_{n+1,k}
for all
k
).
However, as mentioned in the remarks following Hall & Heyde
[
27
, Corollary 3.1 pp. 58-59]
,
since the limit in Assumption
(d)
is deterministic, the conclusion of
[
27
, Corollary 3.1 pp. 58-59]

applies
and
M_{n}
converges in law to a centered Gaussian r.v. with variance
s
. However, this sole convergence does not imply (
3.11
). A related
type of result can be found in Eagleson
[
24
, Corollary 2 p. 560]
, which entails that
{\bf P}
-a.s.

\lim_{n\to\infty}{\bf E}\big[e^{i\lambda M_{n}}\big|\mathcal{G}\big]\!=\!e^{-\frac{1}{2}s\lambda^{2}}
where
\mathcal{G}\!=\!\!=\!\bigcap_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{F}_{n,0}
. However, it does not imply (
3.11
) and the methods used in
[
24
]
seem difficult to adapt in order to obtain (
3.11
). Since we have not found an accurate reference for Proposition
3.5
, we provide in Appendix
C
a proof which is a (relatively straightforward) adaptation of proof of the standard Lindeberg CLT for martingales.
\hfill\square

Proposition 3.7
.

Let
\nu
be a supercritical offspring distribution whose mean is denoted by
\mathtt{m}_{\nu}
.
Let
\mathtt{b}\!\in\!(2,\infty)
. We assume that
\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}k^{\mathtt{b}}\nu(k)\!<\!\infty
.
Let
(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})
be an invariant GW(
\nu
)-tree as in Definition
2.21
.
Let the r.v.s
U_{x},S_{x}
,
x\!\in\!\mathbf{T}
, be as in (
3.2
) and (
3.3
).
Let
\overline{X}_{n}\!=\!(\mathbf{T},X_{n})
,
n\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
, be a
\mathtt{m}_{\nu}
-biased RW on
\mathbf{T}
such that
X_{0}\!=\!\mathtt{o}
.
We denote by
\mathscr{F}_{n}
the
\sigma
-field generated by

(\overline{X}_{k})_{0\leq k\leq n}
. Let
r_{n},s_{n}\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
,
n\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
be such that
\frac{1}{n}r_{n}\!\to\!r\!\in\!\mathbb{R}_{+}
and
\frac{1}{n}s_{n}\!\to\!s\!\in\!\mathbb{R}_{+}
. Recall from (
3.6
) the definition of
\sigma_{\nu}
.
Then for all
\lambda\!\in\!\mathbb{R}
,

\textrm{${\bf P}$-a.s.\penalty\ }\quad\lim_{n\to\infty}{\bf E}\Big[\exp\big(i\tfrac{\lambda}{\sqrt{n}}\big(S_{X_{r_{n}+s_{n}}}\!\!-\!S_{X_{r_{n}}}\big)\big)\Big|\mathscr{F}_{r_{n}}\Big]\!=\!e^{-\frac{{}_{1}}{{}^{2}}s\sigma^{2}_{\nu}\lambda^{2}}\;.

(3.12)

Proof.
We want to apply Proposition
3.5
with
\mathcal{F}_{n,k}\!=\!\mathscr{F}_{r_{n}+k}
and
D_{n,k}\!=\!n^{-1/2}\Delta_{r_{n}+k-1}
, where as in Lemma
3.1

(iv)
, we have set
\Delta_{m}\!=\!S_{X_{m+1}}\!-\!S_{X_{m}}
. Since
\nu
is square integrable, (
3.6
) in Lemma
3.1

(iv)
implies that
{\bf E}[D_{n,k}^{2}]\!=\!\tfrac{1}{n}\sigma_{\nu}^{2}\!<\!\infty
and Lemma
3.1

(iii)
implies that Assumption
(a)
of Proposition
3.5
is satisfied.
Next observe that
\mathcal{F}_{n,0}\!=\!\mathscr{F}_{r_{n}}
: Assumption
(b)
of Proposition
3.5
is satisfied.

We next prove that Assumption
(d)
in Proposition
3.5
is satisfied.
Recall notation

\Phi_{\mathtt{b}}(\overline{X}_{n})
from Lemma
3.1

(iv)
and to simplify notation, we set
C_{\mathtt{b},k}\!=\!\sum_{0\leq l<k}\Phi_{\mathtt{b}}(\overline{X}_{l})
.
First observe that

\tfrac{1}{n}\Phi_{2}(\overline{X}_{r_{n}+k-1})\!=\!\!=\!{\bf E}[D^{2}_{n,k}|\mathcal{F}_{n,k-1}]
.
Since
\Phi_{2}(\overline{X}_{k})\!=\!\Phi_{2}(\mathtt{cent}(\overline{X}_{k}))
by (
3.5
) and
thanks to Corollary
2.23
, we get

{\bf P}
-a.s.
\lim_{k\to\infty}\tfrac{1}{k}C_{2,k}\!=\!\sigma^{2}_{\nu}
.
Recall that
\frac{1}{n}r_{n}\!\to\!r\!\in\!\mathbb{R}_{+}
. If
r\!\in\!(0,\infty)
, then

\tfrac{1}{n}C_{2,r_{n}}\!=\!\tfrac{r_{n}}{n}\tfrac{1}{r_{n}}C_{2,r_{n}}\to r\sigma^{2}_{\nu}
a.s.
If
r\!=\!0
, since
\Phi_{2}\geq 0
, we get for all
\eta\!\in\!(0,1)
,

\tfrac{1}{n}C_{2,r_{n}}\!\leq\!\tfrac{r_{n}+\eta n}{n}\tfrac{1}{r_{n}+\eta n}C_{2,r_{n}+\eta n}\to\eta\sigma^{2}_{\nu}
a.s. and thus
\tfrac{1}{n}C_{2,r_{n}}\to 0
a.s. since
\eta
can be arbitrarily small. Similarly, a.s.
\tfrac{1}{n}C_{2,r_{n}+s_{n}}\to(r+s)\sigma^{2}_{\nu}
, which then implies that

\sum_{1\leq k\leq s_{n}}{\bf E}[D^{2}_{n,k}|\mathcal{F}_{n,k-1}]\!=\!\tfrac{1}{n}(C_{2,r_{n}+s_{n}}\!-\!C_{2,r_{n}})\to s\sigma^{2}_{\nu}
and Assumption
(d)
in Proposition
3.5
is satisfied.

It remains to prove Assumption
(c)
. We fix
\varepsilon\!\in\!(0,1)
and we first
observe that

\sum_{1\leq k\leq s_{n}}\!\!{\bf E}[D_{n,k}^{2}{\bf 1}_{\{|D_{n,k}|>\varepsilon\}}|\mathcal{F}_{n,k-1}]\leq\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{\mathtt{b}-2}}\!\!\sum_{1\leq k\leq s_{n}}\!\!{\bf E}[D_{n,k}^{\mathtt{b}}|\mathcal{F}_{n,k-1}]\leq\tfrac{1}{(\varepsilon\sqrt{n})^{\mathtt{b}-2}}\frac{1}{n}C_{\mathtt{b},r_{n}+s_{n}}

which a.s. tends to
0
since
\frac{1}{n}C_{\mathtt{b},r_{n}+s_{n}}\to(r+s){\bf E}[\Phi_{\mathtt{b}}(\overline{X}_{0})]
a.s. by the ergodic theorem (which applies as Corollary
2.23
since
{\bf E}[\Phi_{\mathtt{b}}(\overline{X}_{0})]\!<\!\infty
by Lemma
3.1

(iv)
). This proves that Assumption
(c)
in Proposition
3.5
is satisfied and (
3.12
) follows from (
3.11
).
\hfill\blacksquare

To conclude this section, let us recall from Peres & Zeitouni
[
45
]
annealed uniform bounds for the (relative) height of
\mathtt{m}_{\nu}
-biased RWs on GW trees which is used later in the proof of Proposition
3.14
in Section
3.5
.

Lemma 3.8
.

Let
\nu
be a supercritical offspring distribution whose mean is denoted by
\mathtt{m}_{\nu}
.
We assume that
\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}k^{2}\nu(k)\!<\!\infty
. Let
(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})
be
either a GW(
\nu
)-tree as in Definition
2.6

(\textbf{a})
(in this case:
\mathtt{o}\!=\!\varnothing
) or an infinite GW(
\nu
)-tree as in Definition
2.16
or an invariant GW(
\nu
)-tree as in
Definition
2.21
. Let
\overline{X}_{n}\!=\!(\mathbf{T},X_{n})
,
n\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
, be a
\mathtt{m}_{\nu}
-biased RW on
\mathbf{T}
such that
X_{0}\!=\!\mathtt{o}
. Then, for all
n,p\!\in\!\mathbb{N}^{*}
, the following holds true.

(i)

If
\mathbf{T}
is a GW(
\nu
)-tree,
{\bf P}\big(\max_{1\leq k\leq n}|X_{k}|\geq p\big)\leq 4ne^{-p^{2}/2n}
.

(ii)

If
\mathbf{T}
is an invariant GW(
\nu
)-tree,
{\bf P}\big(\max_{1\leq k\leq n}\big|\bm{|}\!\!|X_{k}\bm{|}\!\!|\big|\geq p\big)\leq 8n^{3}e^{-(p-1)^{2}/2n}
.

(iii)

If
\mathbf{T}
is an infinite GW(
\nu
)-tree,
{\bf P}\big(\max_{1\leq k\leq n}\big|\bm{|}\!\!|X_{k}\bm{|}\!\!|\big|\geq p\big)\leq 16n^{3}e^{-(p-1)^{2}/2n}
.

Proof.
For
(i)
, see Peres & Zeitouni
[
45
, Lemma 5 p. 608]
. For
(ii)
and
(iii)
, see
[
45
, Corollary 2 p. 608]
.
\hfill\blacksquare

3.2
Statements of the result, overview of its proof

Before stating the main results of the section, let us fix some notation and let us specify our assumptions.
We fix
\alpha\!\in\!(1,2]
and we fix an offspring distribution
\mu
that satisfies (
1.3
).
We denote by

\tau_{\infty}
a GW(
\mu
)-forest corresponding to a sequence
(\tau^{{}_{(k)}}_{{}^{\!}})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
of i.i.d. GW(
\mu
)-trees (Definition
2.6

(\textbf{b})
).
We denote by
V_{l}(\tau_{\infty})
its Lukasiewicz path, by
(H_{s}(\tau_{\infty}))_{s\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}
its height process (Definition
2.2
) and by
(C_{s}(\tau_{\infty}))_{s\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}
its contour process (Definition
2.4
).
We next recall from (
2.58
) the definition of
\mathfrak{g}_{n}
and
\mathfrak{d}_{n}
(roughly speaking
(\mathfrak{g}_{n},\mathfrak{d}_{n})
is the first excursion interval of
H(\tau_{\infty})
above
0
longer than
n
).
Then we also
recall from Theorem
2.30
that
(a_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
and
(b_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
are scaling sequences such that
a_{n}\!=\!n/b_{n}
and such that the following limit

\mathscr{Q}_{n}:=\big(\tfrac{1}{n}\mathfrak{g}_{n},\tfrac{1}{n}\mathfrak{d}_{n},\tfrac{1}{b_{n}}V_{\lfloor n\cdot\rfloor}(\tau_{\infty}),\tfrac{1}{a_{n}}H_{n\cdot}(\tau_{\infty}),\tfrac{1}{a_{n}}C_{2n\cdot}(\tau_{\infty})\big)\underset{n\to\infty}{-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow}\big(\mathbf{g}_{1},\mathbf{d}_{1},X,H,H\big)

(3.13)

holds weakly on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\!\times\!\mathbf{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R})\!\times\!\mathbf{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R})^{2}
.
Here,
X
is an
\alpha
-stable spectrally positive Lévy process characterized by (
2.45
),
H
is its associated height process as defined in (
2.46
) and
(\mathbf{g}_{1},\mathbf{d}_{1})
is the first excursion interval of
H
longer than

1
as defined in Remark
2.28
.

We next state a limit theorem for snakes associated with
\tau_{\infty}
-indexed BRWs that take their values in an invariant GW(
\nu
)-tree (Definition
2.21
). Here
\nu
is a fixed
supercritical
offspring distribution whose mean is denoted by
\mathtt{m}_{\nu}
and which shall be subject to various moment assumptions.

Our main objects of interest in this section are BRWs in invariant environment. More precisely,
we denote by
(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})
an invariant GW(
\nu
)-tree as in Definition
2.21
. We assume that
(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})
is
independent from
\tau_{\infty}
and we introduce the sequence of

\mathbf{T}
-valued BRWs
\bm{\Theta}_{k}\!=\!\big(\tau^{{}_{(k)}}_{{}^{\,}}\!\!\!,\,\varnothing;\overline{Y}^{{}_{(k)}}_{u}\!=\!(\mathbf{T},Y^{{}_{(k)}}_{u}),u\!\in\!\tau^{{}_{(k)}}_{{}^{\!}}\big)
,
k\!\in\!\mathbb{N}^{*}
such that

\textrm{conditionally on $(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})$ and $\tau_{\infty}$, the $(\bm{\Theta}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}$ are independent and}\;\bm{\Theta}_{k}\overset{\textrm{(law)}}{=}Q_{(\tau^{{(k)}},\varnothing)}^{(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})}.

(3.14)

Here
Q_{{}^{(\tau^{{(k)}},\varnothing)}}^{{}_{(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})}}
is as in Definition
2.25
with
\lambda\!=\!\mathtt{m}_{\nu}
.
We recall from (
3.3
) the definition of the harmonic coordinates
(S_{x})_{x\in\mathbf{T}}
.
Then, for all
k\!\in\!\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we associate with
\bm{\Theta}_{k}
two
\mathbb{R}
-valued
\tau^{{}_{(k)}}_{{}^{\!}}
-indexed BRWs:

M_{k}:=\big(\bm{|}\!\!|Y^{{}_{(k)}}_{u}\bm{|}\!\!|\big)_{u\in\tau^{{}_{(k)}}_{{}^{\!}}}\quad\textrm{and}\quad\mathcal{M}_{k}:=\big(S_{Y^{{}_{(k)}}_{u}}\big)_{u\in\tau^{{}_{(k)}}_{{}^{\!}}}\;,

and according to Definition
2.8
, we introduce the following four
\mathbf{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R})
-valued continuous processes:

-

\big(W_{s}(\tau_{\infty};\cdot)\big)_{\!s\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}
is the contour snake of the BRWs
(M^{{}_{(k)}}_{{}^{\!}})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
;

-

\big(W_{s}^{*}(\tau_{\infty};\cdot)\big)_{\!s\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}
is the height snake of the BRWs
(M^{{}_{(k)}}_{{}^{\!}})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
;

-

\big(\mathcal{W}_{s}(\tau_{\infty};\cdot)\big)_{\!s\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}
is the contour snake of the BRWs
(\mathcal{M}^{{}_{(k)}}_{{}^{\!}})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
;

-

\big(\mathcal{W}_{s}^{*}(\tau_{\infty};\cdot)\big)_{\!s\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}
is the height snake of the BRWs
(\mathcal{M}^{{}_{(k)}}_{{}^{\!}})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
.

We also recall from Definition
2.8
the notation
\widehat{W}_{s}(\tau_{\infty})
,
\widehat{W}_{s}^{*}(\tau_{\infty})
,
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{s}(\tau_{\infty})
and
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{s}^{*}(\tau_{\infty})
,
s\!\in\!\mathbb{R}_{+}
, for the corresponding endpoint-processes.

Finally, we denote by
(\widehat{W}_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}
the endpoint process of the one-dimensional Brownian snake whose lifetime-process is the
\alpha
-stable height process
(H_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}
as introduced in Proposition
2.41
.

The goal of this section is to prove the following theorem that shows that the
contour and heigh-snakes in harmonic coordinates and in relative heights jointly converge to
W
when suitably rescaled.
To simplify the proof we first admit several intermediate results, which are stated below and
proved in Sections
3.3
,
3.4
and
3.5
, before proceeding
to the proof of Theorem
3.9
at the end of the present section.

Theorem 3.9
.

We fix
\alpha\!\in\!(1,2]
. Let
\mu
satisfy (
1.3
). Let
\tau_{\infty}
be a GW(
\mu
)-forest (Definition
2.6

(\textbf{b})
). Let
\nu
be a supercritical offspring distribution whose mean is denoted by
\mathtt{m}_{\nu}
. We assume that there is
\mathtt{b}\!\in\!(\frac{2\alpha}{\alpha-1},\infty)
such that
\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}k^{1+2\mathtt{b}}\nu(k)\!<\!\infty
. Let
(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})
be an invariant GW(
\nu
)-tree (Definition
2.21
) that is independent from
\tau_{\infty}
.
We recall the definition of
\mathscr{Q}_{n}
from the convergence (
3.13
). We recall
from (
3.6
) the definition of
\sigma_{\nu}
. Otherwise, we keep the previous notation.
Then, conditionally given
(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})
, the following joint convergence

\displaystyle\Big(\mathscr{Q}_{n},\tfrac{1}{\sigma_{\nu}\!\sqrt{a_{n}}}\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{2n\cdot}(\tau_{\infty}),\tfrac{1}{\sigma_{\nu}\!\sqrt{a_{n}}}\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{*}_{n\cdot}(\tau_{\infty})\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!

,

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\tfrac{\sigma_{\nu}}{\sqrt{a_{n}}}\widehat{W}_{2n\cdot}(\tau_{\infty}),\!\tfrac{\sigma_{\nu}}{\sqrt{a_{n}}}\widehat{W}^{*}_{n\cdot}(\tau_{\infty})\Big)

(3.15)

\displaystyle\underset{n\to\infty}{-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle\big(\mathbf{g}_{1},\mathbf{d}_{1},X,H,H,\widehat{W},\widehat{W},\widehat{W},\widehat{W}\big)

a.s. holds weakly on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\!\times\mathbf{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R})\!\times\!\mathbf{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R})^{6}
equipped with the product topology.

Proof.
See the end of the section.
\hfill\blacksquare

Overview of the proof of Theorem
3.9
.
To prove Theorem
3.9
, we first prove the following proposition in Section
3.4
, which shows that the contour and heigh-snakes in harmonic coordinates converge to
W
when suitably rescaled.

Proposition 3.10
.

We fix
\alpha\!\in\!(1,2]
. Let
\mu
satisfy (
1.3
). Let
\tau_{\infty}
be a GW(
\mu
)-forest (Definition
2.6

(\textbf{b})
). Let
\nu
be a supercritical offspring distribution whose mean is denoted by
\mathtt{m}_{\nu}
. We assume that there is
\mathtt{b}_{0}\!\in\!(1+\frac{\alpha}{\alpha-1},\infty)
such that
\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}k^{1+2\mathtt{b}_{0}}\nu(k)\!<\!\infty
. Let
(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})
be an invariant GW(
\nu
)-tree (Definition
2.21
) that is independent from
\tau_{\infty}
.
We recall the definition of
\mathscr{Q}_{n}
from the convergence (
3.13
). We recall
from (
3.6
) the definition of
\sigma_{\nu}
. Otherwise, we keep the previous notation.
Then, conditionally given
(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})
, the following convergence

\Big(\mathscr{Q}_{n},\tfrac{1}{\sigma_{\nu}\sqrt{a_{n}}}\,\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{2n\cdot}(\tau_{\infty}),\tfrac{1}{\sigma_{\nu}\sqrt{a_{n}}}\,\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{*}_{n\cdot}(\tau_{\infty})\Big)\underset{n\to\infty}{-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow}\big(\mathbf{g}_{1},\mathbf{d}_{1},X,H,H,\widehat{W},\widehat{W}\big)

(3.16)

a.s. holds weakly on
\mathbf{R}^{2}\!\times\!\mathbf{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R})\!\times\!\mathbf{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R})^{4}
equipped with the product topology.

Proof.
See Section
3.4
.
\hfill\blacksquare

To prove Proposition
3.10
, we first rely on the following lemma, which is a conditional version of Kolmogorov continuity theorem and whose proof is a straightforward adaptation of the standard version of the theorem. To state it let us introduce the following notation for the
\gamma
-Hölder factor (here
\gamma\!\in\!(0,1]
) of a function
\mathtt{x}(\cdot)\!\in\!\mathbf{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R})
on the interval
[0,s_{0}]
(here
s_{0}\!\in\!(0,\infty))
:

\mathtt{Hol}_{\gamma,s_{0}}(\mathtt{x})=\sup\Big\{\frac{|\mathtt{x}(s)\!-\!\mathtt{x}(s^{\prime})|}{|s\!-\!s^{\prime}|^{\gamma}}\,;\,s,s^{\prime}\!\in\![0,s_{0}],\;s\!\neq\!s^{\prime}\Big\}

(3.17)

which may be infinite.

Lemma 3.11
.

Let
\mathscr{G}\!\subset\!\mathscr{F}
be a
\sigma
-field and let
(Z_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}
be a
\mathbb{R}
-valued continuous process. Let
s_{0},\mathtt{b}\!\in\!(1,\infty)
and
\mathtt{c}\!\in\!(0,\infty)
. We assume that there is
a
\mathscr{G}
-measurable and a.s. finite r.v.
C
such that for all
s,s^{\prime}\!\in\![0,s_{0}]
,

\textrm{${\bf P}$-a.s.\penalty\ }\quad{\bf E}\Big[\big|Z_{s}\!-\!Z_{s^{\prime}}\big|^{\mathtt{b}}\Big|\,\mathscr{G}\Big]\leq C|s\!-\!s^{\prime}|^{1+\mathtt{c}}.

(3.18)

Then, for all
\gamma\!\in\!(0,\frac{\mathtt{c}}{\mathtt{b}})
, we get

\textrm{${\bf P}$-a.s.\penalty\ }\quad{\bf E}\big[\big(\mathtt{Hol}_{\gamma,s_{0}}(Z)\big)^{\mathtt{b}}\big|\mathscr{G}\big]\leq c_{\mathtt{b},\mathtt{c},\gamma,s_{0}}C,

(3.19)

where
c_{\mathtt{b},\mathtt{c},\gamma,s_{0}}\!=\!2^{2\mathtt{b}}(2s_{0})^{1+c-\gamma b}\big(1\!-\!2^{-(\frac{\mathtt{c}}{\mathtt{b}})+\gamma}\big)^{-\mathtt{b}}
.

Proof:
This is easily adapted from e.g. Revuz & Yor
[
46
, Theorem 2.1 p.26]
.
\hfill\blacksquare

More precisely, in Proposition
3.17
in Section
3.3
, we first show that the rescaled height process
a_{n}^{-1}H_{n\cdot}(\tau_{\infty})
satisfies an (unconditioned) assumption of type (
3.18
). Then in Proposition
3.18
in Section
3.3
, we prove that it entails that

a_{n}^{-1/2}\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{*}_{n\cdot}(\tau_{\infty})
also satisfies an assumption of type (
3.18
). This implies that conditionally given
(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})
,
a_{n}^{-1/2}\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{*}_{n\cdot}(\tau_{\infty})
is tight, thanks to the following general result (the proof given below makes precise what we mean here by conditional tightness).

Lemma 3.12
.

Let
\mathscr{G}\!\subset\!\mathscr{F}
be a
\sigma
-field and let
(Z^{{}_{(n)}}_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}
,
n\!\in\!\mathbb{N}^{*}
, be a sequence of
\mathbb{R}
-valued continuous processes such that
Z^{{}_{(n)}}_{0}\!=\!0
. Let
\mathtt{b}\!\in\!(1,\infty)
and
\gamma\!\in\!(0,1]
. Let us assume that for all
p\!\in\!\mathbb{N}^{*}
, there exists a
\mathscr{G}
-measurable r.v.
\mathtt{C}_{p}
such that for all
n\!\in\!\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

\textrm{${\bf P}$-a.s.\penalty\ }\quad{\bf E}\big[\big(\mathtt{Hol}_{\gamma,\,p}(Z^{{}_{(n)}}_{{}^{\,}})\big)^{\mathtt{b}}\big|\mathscr{G}\big]\leq\mathtt{C}_{p}<\infty\;.

(3.20)

Then conditionally given
\mathscr{G}
, the laws of the processes
(Z^{{}_{(n)}}_{{}^{\,}}\!)_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
are a.s. tight on
\mathbf{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R})
.

Proof.
We denote by
\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{C}^{{}_{0}}_{{}^{1}})
the space of Borel probability measures on
\mathbf{C}^{{}_{0}}_{{}^{1}}\!:=\!\mathbf{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R})
equipped with the Polish topology of weak convergence. Denote by
\omega\!\in\!\Omega\!\mapsto\!Q_{n}(\omega,\cdot)
a regular version of the conditional law of
Z^{{}_{(n)}}_{{}^{\,}}
given
\mathscr{G}
(which exists
since
\mathbf{C}^{{}_{0}}_{{}^{1}}
is Polish). Namely,

-

Q_{n}
is a
\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{C}^{{}_{0}}_{{}^{1}})
-valued r.v., measurable with respect to
\mathscr{G}
and the Borel
\sigma
-field of
\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{C}^{{}_{0}}_{{}^{1}})
.

-

For all Borel subsets
B
of

\mathbf{C}^{{}_{0}}_{{}^{1}}
we
{\bf P}
-a.s. have
Q_{n}(\cdot,B)\!=\!{\bf E}[{\bf 1}_{\{Z^{(n)}\in B\}}|\mathscr{G}]
.

Our assumptions imply that there exists
\Omega_{0}\!\in\!\mathscr{G}
such that
{\bf P}(\Omega_{0})\!=\!1
and such that for all
\omega\!\in\!\Omega_{0}
and for all
n,p\!\in\!\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

{\bf E}\big[\big(\mathtt{Hol}_{\gamma,\,p}(Z^{{}_{(n)}}_{{}^{\,}})\big)^{\mathtt{b}}\big|\mathscr{G}\big](\omega)\,=\int_{\mathbf{C}^{0}_{1}}\!\!\!\big(\mathtt{Hol}_{\gamma,\,p}(\mathtt{x})\big)^{\mathtt{b}}Q_{n}(\omega,d\mathtt{x})\!\leq\!\mathtt{C}_{p}(\omega)<\infty\;.

For all
\mathtt{x}(\cdot)\!\in\!\mathbf{C}^{{}_{0}}_{{}^{1}}
and
\delta\!\in\!(0,\infty)
, we set
w_{\delta}(\mathtt{x},p)\!=\!\max\big\{|\mathtt{x}(s)\!-\!\mathtt{x}(s^{\prime})|\,;\,s,s^{\prime}\!\in\![0,p]:|s\!-\!s^{\prime}|\!\leq\!\delta\big\}
, which is the
\delta
-modulus of continuity of
\mathtt{x}
on
[0,p]
. Then, we get for all
\omega\!\in\!\Omega_{0}
, all
p,n\!\in\!\mathbb{N}^{*}
and all
\delta,\eta\!\in\!(0,\infty)
,

Q_{n}\big(\omega\,,\,\big\{\mathtt{x}\!\in\!\mathbf{C}^{{}_{0}}_{{}^{1}}:w_{\delta}(\mathtt{x},p)\!>\!\eta\big\}\big)\leq\frac{\delta^{\gamma\mathtt{b}}}{\eta^{\mathtt{b}}}\int_{\mathbf{C}^{0}_{1}}\!\!\!\big(\mathtt{Hol}_{\gamma,\,p}(\mathtt{x})\big)^{\mathtt{b}}Q_{n}(\omega,d\mathtt{x})\!\leq\!\mathtt{C}_{p}(\omega)\frac{\delta^{\gamma\mathtt{b}}}{\eta^{\mathtt{b}}},

which implies
\lim_{\delta\to 0^{+}}\limsup_{n\to\infty}Q_{n}\big(\omega,\big\{\mathtt{x}\!\in\!\mathbf{C}^{{}_{0}}_{{}^{1}}:w_{\delta}(\mathtt{x},p)\!>\!\eta\big\}\big)\!=\!0
. Since
Z^{{}_{(n)}}_{0}\!=\!0
, a standard result (see e.g. Billingsley
[
8
, Theorem 7.3 p. 82]
) entails that for all
\omega\!\in\!\Omega_{0}
, the laws
(Q_{n}(\omega,\cdot))_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
are tight.
\hfill\blacksquare

In Section
3.4
, we prove that
a_{n}^{-1}H_{n\cdot}(\tau_{\infty})
and
\sigma^{-1}_{\nu}a_{n}^{-1/2}\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{*}_{n\cdot}(\tau_{\infty})
jointly converge in law conditionally given
(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})
to
(H,\widehat{W})
. To do so, we compute the conditional Fourier transforms of finite-dimensional marginals of the height snake in harmonic coordinates and we use Proposition
3.7
.

We next derive the convergence (
3.16
) using the fact that
C(\tau_{\infty})
and
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}(\tau_{\infty})
are derived
from
H(\tau_{\infty})
and
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{*}(\tau_{\infty})
by a time-change.
More precisely, recall from Remark
2.5

(\textbf{d})
the definition of the increasing continuous bijection
\phi_{\tau_{\infty}}\!:\!\mathbb{R}_{+}\!\to\!\mathbb{R}_{+}
such that
H_{s}(\tau_{\infty})\!=\!C_{\phi_{\tau_{\infty}}(s)}(\tau_{\infty})
,
s\!\in\!\mathbb{R}_{+}
. For all
s_{0}\!\in\!(0,\infty)
, (
2.9
) implies that

\max_{s\in[0,s_{0}]}|\frac{1}{2n}\phi_{\tau_{\infty}}(ns)\!-\!s|\to 0
in probability because
(
3.13
) implies that
\frac{1}{n}\max_{s\in[0,s_{0}]}H_{ns}(\tau_{\infty})\to 0
in
{\bf P}
-probability since
a_{n}/n\to 0
. Therefore, if for all
s\!\in\!\mathbb{R}_{+}
we set
\varphi_{n}(s)\!=\!\frac{1}{n}\phi^{-1}_{\tau_{\infty}}(2ns)
(here,
\phi^{-1}_{\tau_{\infty}}
stands for the inverse of
\phi_{\tau_{\infty}}
), then
we have proved

\forall s_{0}\!\in\!(0,\infty),\quad\lim_{n\to\infty}\max_{s\in(0,s_{0}]}\big|\varphi_{n}(s)\!-\!s\big|=0\quad\textrm{in ${\bf P}$-probability.}

(3.21)

We derive the conditional joint convergence (
3.16
)

-
from
the conditional joint convergence of
a_{n}^{-1}H_{n\cdot}(\tau_{\infty})
and
\sigma_{\nu}^{-1}a_{n}^{-1/2}\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{*}_{n\cdot}(\tau_{\infty})
,

-
from the following equalities that holds for all
s\!\in\!\mathbb{R}_{+}

\tfrac{1}{a_{n}}C_{2ns}(\tau_{\infty})\!=\!\tfrac{1}{a_{n}}H_{n\varphi_{n}(s)}(\tau_{\infty})\quad\textrm{and}\quad\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{a_{n}}}\,\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{2ns}(\tau_{\infty})=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{a_{n}}}\,\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{*}_{n\varphi_{n}(s)}(\tau_{\infty})\;,

(3.22)

-
from the following deterministic lemma.

Lemma 3.13
.

Suppose that
\mathtt{x}_{n}\!\rightarrow\!\mathtt{x}
in
\mathbf{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}^{q})
and that

\varphi_{n}\!\rightarrow\!\varphi
in
\mathbf{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}_{+})
. Assume that

\varphi_{n}(0)\!=\!0
and that
\varphi_{n}
is nondecreasing. Then,

\mathtt{x}_{n}\circ\varphi_{n}\!\rightarrow\!\mathtt{x}\circ\varphi
in
\mathbf{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}^{q})
.

Proof.
See Whitt
[
50
, Theorem 3.1, p. 75]
.
\hfill\blacksquare

Proposition 3.14
.

We fix
\alpha\!\in\!(1,2]
. Let
\mu
satisfy (
1.3
). Let
\tau_{\infty}
be a GW(
\mu
)-forest (Definition
2.6

(\textbf{b})
). Let
\nu
be a supercritical offspring distribution whose mean is denoted by
\mathtt{m}_{\nu}
. We assume that there is
\mathtt{b}\!\in\!(\frac{2\alpha}{\alpha-1},\infty)
such that
\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}k^{1+2\mathtt{b}}\nu(k)\!<\!\infty
.
We keep the notation of Proposition
3.10
. Let
s_{0}\in(0,\infty)
. Then there are two constants
c,\varepsilon_{1}\!\in\!(0,\infty)
, which only depend on
\mu
,
\nu
,
\mathtt{b}
and
s_{0}
, such that

\textrm{${\bf P}$-a.s.\penalty\ }\quad\lim_{n\to\infty}{\bf P}\Big(\!\!\!\!\max_{\hskip 8.19447pts\in[0,s_{0}]}\!\big|\tfrac{1}{\sigma^{2}_{\nu}}\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{*}_{ns}(\tau_{\infty})\!-\!\widehat{W}^{*}_{ns}(\tau_{\infty})\big|\,>\,cn^{-\varepsilon_{1}}\sqrt{a_{n}}\,\Big|\,(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})\Big)=0\;.

(3.23)

Proof.
See Section
3.5
.
\hfill\blacksquare

Proof of Theorem
3.9
.
We admit Propositions
3.10
and
3.14
and we prove Theorem
3.9
. First note that the moment
assumption on
\nu
is stronger in Proposition
3.14
(and Theorem
3.9
) than in Proposition
3.10
since
\frac{2\alpha}{\alpha-1}\!>\!1+\frac{\alpha}{\alpha-1}
. Thus under the assumptions of Theorem
3.9
, both propositions apply and they
easily entail that conditionally given
(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})
, the following convergence

\big(\mathscr{Q}_{n},\tfrac{1}{a_{n}}H_{n\cdot}(\tau_{\infty}),\tfrac{1}{\sigma_{\nu}\sqrt{a_{n}}}\,\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{*}_{n\cdot}(\tau_{\infty}),\tfrac{\sigma_{\nu}}{\sqrt{a_{n}}}\,\widehat{W}^{*}_{n\cdot}(\tau_{\infty})\big)\underset{n\to\infty}{-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow}\big(\mathbf{g}_{1},\mathbf{d}_{1},X,H,H,H,\widehat{W},\widehat{W}\big)

(3.24)

holds weakly on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\!\times\!\mathbf{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R})\!\times\!\mathbf{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R})^{5}
equipped with the product topology.
Then recall (
3.21
), (
3.22
) and recall (by definition of height snake) that for all
s\!\in\!\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

\tfrac{1}{a_{n}}C_{2ns}(\tau_{\infty})=\tfrac{1}{a_{n}}H_{n\varphi_{n}(s)}(\tau_{\infty})\quad\textrm{and}\quad\tfrac{\sigma_{\nu}}{\sqrt{a_{n}}}\,\widehat{W}_{2ns}(\tau_{\infty})=\tfrac{\sigma_{\nu}}{\sqrt{a_{n}}}\,\widehat{W}^{*}_{n\varphi_{n}(s)}(\tau_{\infty})\;.

This entails (
3.15
) by (
3.24
) and Lemma
3.13
, which completes the proof of Theorem
3.9
.
\hfill\blacksquare

3.3
Quenched tightness of the height snake of
\tau_{\infty}
-indexed BRWs

We keep the notation and the general assumptions of the previous section.
We first prove Hölder estimates for
H(\tau_{\infty})
by adapting to the discrete setting the proof of D. & Le Gall
[
21
, Lemma 1.4.6]
which provides a similar result for the continuous height process.

To that end, we use the fact that the Lukasiewicz path
V(\tau_{\infty})
of
\tau_{\infty}

is a RW whose jump law is
\widetilde{\mu}(k)\!=\!\mu(k+1)
,
k\!\in\!\mathbb{N}\cup\{-1\}
(Remark
2.7
(
\mathbf{b}
)) and we use (
2.4
)
which shows that
H_{n}(\tau_{\infty})
counts the number of times the time-reversed RW
(V_{n}(\tau_{\infty})\!-\!V_{n-k}(\tau_{\infty}))_{0\leq k\leq n}

reaches its supremum. Namely, the height process is related to the so-called
weak ascending ladder variables
of
V(\tau_{\infty})
whose definition and basic properties are recalled from Spitzer
[
48
]
below.
Let
(V_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a RW whose jump law is
\widetilde{\mu}
. For all
n\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
, we set

\overline{V}_{\!n}\!=\!\max_{0\leq k\leq n}V_{k}\quad\textrm{and}\quad\mathtt{\mathtt{}}L_{n}\!=\!\#\{k\!\in\!\{1,\ldots,n\}:V_{k}\!=\!\overline{V}_{\!k}\}\;,

(3.25)

with the convention that
L_{0}\!=\!0
.
The weak ascending ladder variables are then given by

\mathcal{L}_{n}=\inf\{k\!\in\!\mathbb{N}:L_{k}=n\}\quad\textrm{and}\quad{\mathcal{V}}_{n}=V_{\mathcal{L}_{n}}=\overline{V}_{\!\!\!\mathcal{L}_{n}}.

(3.26)

We also recall from (
2.67
) that
T_{-n}\!=\!\inf\{k\!\in\!\mathbb{N}:V_{k}\!=\!-n\}
. We recall from (
2.56
) that

g_{\mu}
is the generating function of
\mu
and we recall from Lemma
2.32
that
\varphi_{\mu}
is the generating function of
T_{-1}
. Then
(\mathcal{L}_{n},{\mathcal{V}}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is a
\mathbb{N}^{2}
-valued RW whose jump law is characterized by

\forall r,s\!\in\![0,1],\quad 1-{\bf E}\big[r^{\mathcal{L}_{1}}s^{{\mathcal{V}}_{1}}\big]=\frac{s-rg_{\mu}(s)}{s-\varphi_{\mu}(r)}\;.

(3.27)

For a proof we refer to Spitzer
[
48
]
, Chapter IV,
P5
(b) p. 181 and more precisely to (3) p. 187 in the specific case of left-continuous RWs (with Spitzer’s notation,
P\!=\!g_{\mu}
and
r\!=\!\varphi_{\mu}
). We next provide estimates on the Laplace exponents of
\mathcal{L}_{1}
,
{\mathcal{V}}_{1}
and
T_{-1}
.

Lemma 3.15
.

We keep the above notation. We fix
\alpha\!\in\!(1,2]
and we assume that
\mu
satisfies (
1.3
). For all
\lambda\!\in\!\mathbb{R}_{+}
, we set
\Lambda_{0}(\lambda)\!=\!-\log{\bf E}[e^{-\lambda\mathcal{L}_{1}}]
,
\Lambda_{1}(\lambda)\!=\!-\log\varphi_{\mu}(e^{-\lambda})
and
\Lambda_{2}(\lambda)\!=\!-\log{\bf E}[e^{-\lambda{\mathcal{V}}_{1}}]
. Then there are
c_{\mu},c^{\prime}_{\mu}\!\in\!(0,\infty)
such that

\Lambda_{0}(\lambda)\sim_{0^{+}}c_{\mu}\,\lambda^{\frac{\!\alpha-1}{\alpha}}L\big(\tfrac{1}{\lambda}\big)\quad\textrm{and}\quad\Lambda_{2}(\Lambda_{1}(\lambda))\sim_{0^{+}}c^{\prime}_{\mu}\,\lambda^{\frac{\!\alpha-1}{\alpha}}L\big(\tfrac{1}{\lambda}\big)

(3.28)

where
L
is the slowly varying function appearing in (
2.54
).

Proof.
By (
3.27
),
1\!-\!{\bf E}[e^{-\lambda\mathcal{L}_{1}}]\!=\!(1\!-\!e^{-\lambda})/(1\!-\!\varphi_{\mu}(e^{-\lambda}))
. Then the first equivalence in (
3.28
) follows from (
2.68
) in Lemma
2.32
. Next, we deduce from (
3.27
) that
1\!-\!{\bf E}[e^{-\lambda{\mathcal{V}}_{1}}]\!=\!\psi_{\mu}(s_{\lambda})/s_{\lambda}
. where we recall

\psi_{\mu}
from (
2.56
) and where we have set
s_{\lambda}\!=\!1\!-\!e^{-\lambda}
. Then,
\Lambda_{2}(\lambda)\sim_{0^{+}}c_{\alpha}^{\prime}\lambda^{\alpha-1}L^{*}(1/\lambda)
where
L^{*}
is the slowly varying function in (
2.55
).
Then (
2.68
) in Lemma
2.32
entails that

\Lambda_{2}(\Lambda_{1}(\lambda))\sim_{0^{+}}c_{1}\lambda^{\frac{\alpha-1}{\alpha}}L\big(\tfrac{1}{\lambda}\big)^{-(\alpha-1)}L^{*}\big(c_{2}\lambda^{-\frac{1}{\alpha}}L\big(\tfrac{1}{\lambda}\big)\big)\;,

where
c_{1},c_{2}\!\in\!(0,\infty)
are two constants which only depend on
\alpha
. This implies the second equivalent in (
3.28
) by (
2.57
), which relates
L
and
L^{*}
.
\hfill\blacksquare

Proposition 3.16
.

We keep the above notation. We assume that
\mu
is non-trivial and critical.
Let
\mathtt{b}\in(1,\infty)
. Then for all
m\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
and
n\!\in\!\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

\displaystyle{\bf E}\Big[\big|H_{m+n}(\tau_{\infty})\!-\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\inf_{\;\hskip 8.19447pt[\![m,m+n]\!]}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!H(\tau_{\infty})\big|^{\mathtt{b}}\Big]

\displaystyle\!\leq\!

\displaystyle e\mathtt{b}\Gamma_{\!\!\mathtt{e}}(\mathtt{b})\,\big(\Lambda_{0}\big(\tfrac{1}{n}\big)\big)^{-\mathtt{b}}\qquad\textrm{and}

\displaystyle{\bf E}\Big[\big|H_{m}(\tau_{\infty})\!-\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\inf_{\;\hskip 8.19447pt[\![m,m+n]\!]}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!H(\tau_{\infty})\big|^{\mathtt{b}}\Big]

\displaystyle\!\leq\!

\displaystyle e\mathtt{b}\Gamma_{\!\!\mathtt{e}}(\mathtt{b})\,\big(\Lambda_{2}\big(\Lambda_{1}\big(\tfrac{1}{n}\big)\big)\big)^{-\mathtt{b}}.

(3.29)

Proof.
We prove the first inequality in (
3.16
). Without loss of generality, we assume that
V
is a RW whose jump law is
\widetilde{\mu}
such that
V_{k}\!=\!V_{m+n}(\tau_{\infty})\!-\!V_{m+n-k}(\tau_{\infty})
,
0\!\leq\!k\!\leq\!m+n
. Recall from (
3.25
) the notation
L
. We easily deduce from (
2.4
) that

L_{n}\!=\!H_{m+n}(\tau_{\infty})\!-\!\inf_{[\![m,m+n]\!]}H(\tau_{\infty})
. Thus,

\displaystyle{\bf E}\Big[\big|H_{m+n}(\tau_{\infty})\!-\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\inf_{\;\hskip 8.19447pt[\![m,m+n]\!]}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!H(\tau_{\infty})\big|^{\mathtt{b}}\Big]\!\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\mathtt{b}s^{\mathtt{b}-1}{\bf P}\big(L_{n}\!>\!s\big)\,ds\,\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\mathtt{b}s^{\mathtt{b}-1}{\bf P}\big(\mathcal{L}_{\lceil s\rceil}\!\leq\!n\big)\,ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\!\!\!\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\mathtt{b}s^{\mathtt{b}-1}e\cdot{\bf E}\big[\exp\big(\!-\!\tfrac{1}{n}\mathcal{L}_{\lceil s\rceil}\big)\big]ds\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\mathtt{b}s^{\mathtt{b}-1}e\cdot e^{-s\Lambda_{0}(\frac{1}{n})}ds,

which implies the desired result. Here, we use Markov inequality at the second line and the fact that
\mathcal{L}
is a nonnegative RW whose Laplace exponent is
\Lambda_{0}
.

The proof of the second upper bound in (
3.16
) is slightly more involved. To simplify notation we set
I_{k,l}\!=\!\min_{[\![k,l]\!]}V(\tau_{\infty})
, for all integers
l\!\geq\!k\!\geq\!0
. Denote by
m^{\prime}
the smallest integer
k\!\in\![\![m,m+n]\!]
such that
V_{k}(\tau_{\infty})\!=\!I_{m,m+n}
. We deduce from (
2.4
) that

\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\inf_{\;\hskip 8.19447pt[\![m,m+n]\!]}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!H(\tau_{\infty})\!=\!H_{m^{\prime}}(\tau_{\infty})\!=\!\#\big\{l\!\in\![\![0,m-1]\!]:V_{l}(\tau_{\infty})\!=\!I_{l,m}\;\textrm{and}\;I_{l,m}\!\leq\!I_{m,m+n}\}.

We now assume that
V
is such that

V_{k}\!=\!V_{m}(\tau_{\infty})\!-\!V_{m-k}(\tau_{\infty})
,
0\!\leq\!k\!\leq\!m
and we set
V^{\prime}\!=\!(V_{m+k}(\tau_{\infty})\!-\!V_{m}(\tau_{\infty}))_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
that is an independent copy of
V
. We set
I^{\prime}_{n}\!=\!\min_{[\![0,n]\!]}V^{\prime}
. Then, (
2.4
) implies that

H_{m}(\tau_{\infty})\!-\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\inf_{\;\hskip 8.19447pt[\![m,m+n]\!]}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!H(\tau_{\infty})=\#\big\{k\!\in\![\![1,m]\!]:V_{k}\!=\!\overline{V}_{\!k}\!<\!-I_{n}^{\prime}\}=L_{m\wedge(\overline{V}^{{}_{-1}}_{\!\!\!-I_{n}^{\prime}}-1)}\;,

where we have set
\overline{V}^{{}_{-1}}_{\!k}\!=\!\inf\{l\!\in\!\mathbb{N}:\overline{V}_{\!l}\!\geq\!k\}
. We then set
T^{\prime}_{-k}\!=\!\inf\{l\!\in\!\mathbb{N}:V^{\prime}_{l}\!=\!-k\}
and we deduce from the previous inequality that for all integer
k\!\in\!\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

\displaystyle{\bf P}\big(H_{m}(\tau_{\infty})\!-\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\inf_{\;\hskip 8.19447pt[\![m,m+n]\!]}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!H(\tau_{\infty})\!\geq\!k\big)

\displaystyle\!\leq\!

\displaystyle{\bf P}\big(L_{\overline{V}^{{}_{-1}}_{\!\!\!-I_{n}^{\prime}}-1}\!\geq\!k\big)\!=\!{\bf P}\big(\!-\!I_{n}^{\prime}\!>\!{\mathcal{V}}_{k}\big)\!\leq\!{\bf P}\big(T^{\prime}_{\!-{\mathcal{V}}_{k}}\!\leq\!n\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e\cdot{\bf E}\big[\exp\big(\!-\!\tfrac{1}{n}T^{\prime}_{\!-{\mathcal{V}}_{k}}\big)\big]=e\cdot\exp\big(\!-\!k\Lambda_{2}\big(\Lambda_{1}\big(\tfrac{1}{n}\big)\big)\big),

by the Markov inequality. It entails the following,

{\bf E}\Big[\big|H_{m}(\tau_{\infty})\!-\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\inf_{\;\hskip 8.19447pt[\![m,m+n]\!]}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!H(\tau_{\infty})\big|^{\mathtt{b}}\Big]\leq e\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\mathtt{b}s^{\mathtt{b}-1}e^{-\lceil s\rceil\Lambda_{2}(\Lambda_{1}(\frac{1}{n}))}\,ds,

which easily implies the second upper bound in (
3.16
).
\hfill\blacksquare

Proposition 3.17
.

We fix
\alpha\!\in\!(1,2]
and we assume that
\mu
satisfies (
1.3
). Recall from (
2.59
) the definition of
(a_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
.
Let
\mathtt{b}\!\in\!\big(1,\infty\big)
and
s_{0}\!\in\!(1,\infty)
and let
\varepsilon\!\in\!\big(0,1\!-\!\frac{1}{\alpha}\big)
.
Then there exists
c\!\in\!(1,\infty)
that only depends on
\mu,\mathtt{b},s_{0}
and
\varepsilon
such that

\forall n\!\in\!\mathbb{N}^{*},\forall s,s^{\prime}\!\in\![0,s_{0}],\quad{\bf E}\Big[\big|\tfrac{1}{a_{n}}H_{ns^{\prime}}(\tau_{\infty})\!-\!\tfrac{1}{a_{n}}H_{ns}(\tau_{\infty})\big|^{\mathtt{b}}\Big]\leq c\,\big|s^{\prime}\!-\!s\big|^{\mathtt{b}(1-\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon)}\;.

(3.30)

Moreover, for all
\gamma\!\in\!\big(0,1\!-\!\tfrac{1}{\alpha}\big)
, there exists
c^{\prime}\!\in\!(1,\infty)

that only depends on
\mu,\mathtt{b},s_{0}
and
\gamma
such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}{\bf E}\Big[\Big(\mathtt{Hol}_{\gamma,s_{0}}\big(\tfrac{1}{a_{n}}H_{n\cdot}(\tau_{\infty})\big)\Big)^{\!\mathtt{b}}\Big]\leq c^{\prime},

(3.31)

where we recall from (
3.17
) the definition of
\mathtt{Hol}_{\gamma,s_{0}}(\mathtt{x}(\cdot))
, for all
\mathtt{x}\!\in\!\mathbf{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R})
. In particular it implies that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}{\bf E}\Big[\Big(\max_{s\in[0,s_{0}]}\tfrac{1}{a_{n}}H_{ns}(\tau_{\infty})\Big)^{\!\mathtt{b}}\Big]\leq c^{\prime}s_{0}^{\gamma}.

(3.32)

Proof.
We set
\mathtt{a}(x)=x^{1-\frac{1}{\alpha}}/L(x)
where
L
is as in (
2.54
). By convenience, we set
\mathtt{a}(0)\!=\!0
. Thus
\mathtt{a}(n)\!=\!a_{n}
by (
2.59
). To simplify notation, we set
\mathcal{H}_{s}\!=\!H_{s}(\tau_{\infty})
,
s\!\in\!\mathbb{R}_{+}
. By (
3.16
) and Lemma
3.15
, there exists a constant
c_{1}(\mu,\mathtt{b})\!\in\!(1,\infty)
(that only depends on
\mu
and
\mathtt{b}
) such that for all integers
m\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
and
n\!\in\!\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

{\bf E}\Big[\big|\mathcal{H}_{m+n}-\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\inf_{\;\hskip 8.19447ptk\in[\![m,m+n]\!]}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\mathcal{H}_{k}\;\big|^{\mathtt{b}}\Big]\vee{\bf E}\Big[\big|\mathcal{H}_{m}-\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\inf_{\;\hskip 8.19447ptk\in[\![m,m+n]\!]}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\mathcal{H}_{k}\;\big|^{\mathtt{b}}\Big]\leq c_{1}(\mu,\mathtt{b})\mathtt{a}(n)^{\mathtt{b}}\;.

(3.33)

We first prove an upper bound for
|\mathcal{H}_{s^{\prime}}\!-\!\mathcal{H}_{s}|
that is a simple consequence of the specific interpolation explained by (
2.2
) in Definition
2.2

(\textbf{a})
and that is used when
|s-s^{\prime}|
is small: there exists

c_{2}(\mu,\mathtt{b})\!\in\!(1,\infty)
(that only depends on
\mu
and
\mathtt{b}
) such that for all
s,s^{\prime}\!\in\!\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

{\bf E}\Big[\big|\mathcal{H}_{s^{\prime}}\!-\!\mathcal{H}_{s}\big|^{\mathtt{b}}\Big]\leq c_{2}(\mu,\mathtt{b})\,|s^{\prime}\!-\!s|^{\mathtt{b}}\;.

(3.34)

Proof.
Let us suppose first that there is
m\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
such that

\frac{{}_{1}}{{}^{2}}m\!\leq\!s\!\leq\!s^{\prime}\leq\frac{{}_{1}}{{}^{2}}(m+1)
, and set
n\!=\!\lfloor\frac{{}_{1}}{{}^{2}}m\rfloor
.
Then, (
2.2
) implies that
|\mathcal{H}_{s^{\prime}}\!-\!\mathcal{H}_{s}|\!=\!2(s^{\prime}\!-\!s)|\mathcal{H}_{\frac{m}{2}}-\mathcal{H}_{\frac{m+1}{2}}|\!\leq\!2(s^{\prime}\!-\!s)(1+\mathcal{H}_{n}\!-\!\inf_{k\in[\![n,n+1]\!]}\mathcal{H}_{k})
.

For all real valued r.v.s
X
, we use the notation
\lVert X\rVert_{\mathtt{b}}\!:=\!({\bf E}[|X|^{\mathtt{b}})^{1/\mathtt{b}}
, for the

L^{\mathtt{b}}
norm of
X
.
By (
3.33
), we get
\lVert\mathcal{H}_{s^{\prime}}\!-\!\mathcal{H}_{s}\rVert_{\mathtt{b}}\leq c(s^{\prime}\!-\!s)
where

c\!:=\!2+2c_{1}(\mu,\mathtt{b})^{1/\mathtt{b}}\mathtt{a}(1)
. If
\lfloor 2s\rfloor\!<\!\lfloor 2s^{\prime}\rfloor
, then we get

\displaystyle\lVert\mathcal{H}_{s^{\prime}}\!-\!\mathcal{H}_{s}\rVert_{\mathtt{b}}

\displaystyle\!\leq\!

\displaystyle\lVert\mathcal{H}_{s}\!-\!\mathcal{H}_{\frac{1}{2}\lceil 2s\rceil}\rVert_{\mathtt{b}}+\lVert\mathcal{H}_{s^{\prime}}\!-\!\mathcal{H}_{\frac{1}{2}\lfloor 2s^{\prime}\rfloor}\rVert_{\mathtt{b}}+\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{\hskip 8.19447pt\lceil 2s\rceil\leq k<\lfloor 2s^{\prime}\rfloor}\!\!\!\!\!\!\lVert\mathcal{H}_{\frac{1}{2}(k+1)}\!-\!\mathcal{H}_{\frac{1}{2}k}\rVert_{\mathtt{b}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\lceil 2s\rceil\!-\!s+s^{\prime}\!-\!\tfrac{1}{2}\lfloor 2s^{\prime}\rfloor+\tfrac{1}{2}\lfloor 2s^{\prime}\rfloor\!-\!\tfrac{1}{2}\lceil 2s\rceil\big)=c(s^{\prime}\!-\!s),

which entails (
3.34
).
\hfill\square

Recall the convention
\mathtt{a}(0)\!=\!0
. We next prove the following bound: there exists

c_{3}(\mu,\mathtt{b})\!\in\!(1,\infty)
(that only depends on
\mu
and
\mathtt{b}
) such that for all real numbers
s^{\prime}\!\geq\!s\geq 0
,

{\bf E}\Big[\big|\mathcal{H}_{s^{\prime}}\!-\!\mathcal{H}_{s}\big|^{\mathtt{b}}\Big]\leq c_{3}(\mu,\mathtt{b})\big(1+\mathtt{a}(\lfloor s^{\prime}\rfloor\!-\!\lfloor s\rfloor)\big)^{\mathtt{b}}.

(3.35)

Proof.
By (
3.33
) and (
3.34
), we get

\displaystyle\lVert\mathcal{H}_{s^{\prime}}\!-\!\mathcal{H}_{s}\rVert_{\mathtt{b}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\big|\!\big|\mathcal{H}_{s}\!-\!\mathcal{H}_{\lfloor s\rfloor}\big|\!\big|_{\mathtt{b}}+\big|\!\big|\mathcal{H}_{s^{\prime}}\!-\!\mathcal{H}_{\lfloor s^{\prime}\rfloor}\big|\!\big|_{\mathtt{b}}+\big|\!\big|\mathcal{H}_{\lfloor s\rfloor}-\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\inf_{\;\hskip 8.19447ptk\in[\![\lfloor s\rfloor,\lfloor s^{\prime}\rfloor]\!]}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\mathcal{H}_{k}\;\,\big|\!\big|_{\mathtt{b}}+\big|\!\big|\mathcal{H}_{\lfloor s^{\prime}\rfloor}-\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\inf_{\;\hskip 8.19447ptk\in[\![\lfloor s\rfloor,\lfloor s^{\prime}\rfloor]\!]}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\mathcal{H}_{k}\;\,\big|\!\big|_{\mathtt{b}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2c_{2}(\mu,\mathtt{b})^{1/\mathtt{b}}+2c_{1}(\mu,\mathtt{b})^{1/\mathtt{b}}\mathtt{a}(\lfloor s^{\prime}\rfloor\!-\!\lfloor s\rfloor),

which easily implies (
3.35
).

\hfill\square

We next compare
\mathtt{a}(\cdot)
to power functions thanks to Potter’s bounds. Let
s_{0}
and
\varepsilon
as in the statement of the proposition. There exist
n_{0}\!\in\!\mathbb{N}^{*}
and
c_{4}\!\in\!(1,\infty)
that only depend on
\mu
,
\varepsilon
and
s_{0}
such that for all integer
n\geq n_{0}
and all real number
s\!\in\![\frac{n_{0}}{n},s_{0}]
,

\frac{1}{c_{4}}s^{\varepsilon}\leq\frac{L(n)}{L(ns)}\leq c_{4}\,s^{-\varepsilon}\quad\textrm{and thus}\quad\frac{\mathtt{a}(ns)}{\mathtt{a}(n)}\leq c_{4}\,s^{1-\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon}\;.

(3.36)

Proof.
By Potter’s bounds (see e.g. Bingham, Goldies & Teugel
[
9
, Theorem 1.5.6]
), there exists

n_{0}\!\in\!\mathbb{N}^{*}
that depend on
L
,
\varepsilon
and
s_{0}
such that for all real numbers
y\!\geq\!x\!\geq\!n_{0}
,
L(x)/L(y)
and
L(y)/L(x)
belong to
[\frac{{}_{1}}{{}^{2}}(y/x)^{-\varepsilon},2(y/x)^{\varepsilon}]
. We fix
n\!\geq\!n_{0}
. If
s\!\in\![\frac{n_{0}}{n},1]
, then
L(n)/L(ns)\!\in\![\frac{{}_{1}}{{}^{2}}s^{\varepsilon},2s^{-\varepsilon}]
. If
s\!\in\![1,s_{0}]
, then
L(n)/L(ns)\!\in\![\frac{{}_{1}}{{}^{2}}s^{-\varepsilon},2s^{\varepsilon}]
and we observe that
2s^{\varepsilon}\leq 2s_{0}^{2\varepsilon}s^{-\varepsilon}
. This implies (
3.36
)
by taking
c_{4}\!=\!2s_{0}^{2\varepsilon}
.
\hfill\square

We now complete the proof of (
3.30
): we fix
s,s^{\prime}\!\in\![0,s_{0}]
such that
s^{\prime}\!\geq\!s
and we suppose that
n\geq n_{0}
without loss of generality. We first assume that
s^{\prime}\!-\!s\!\leq\!\frac{n_{0}}{n}
. In this case we use (
3.34
) to get

\lVert\tfrac{1}{a_{n}}\mathcal{H}_{ns^{\prime}}\!-\!\tfrac{1}{a_{n}}\mathcal{H}_{ns}\rVert_{\mathtt{b}}\leq c_{2}(\mu,\!\mathtt{b})^{1/\mathtt{b}}n(s^{\prime}\!-\!s)/a_{n}
. Now observe that

\tfrac{n}{a_{n}}|s^{\prime}\!-\!s|\!\leq\!n^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\!L(n)\big(\tfrac{n_{0}}{n}\big)^{\frac{1}{\alpha}+\varepsilon}|s^{\prime}\!-\!s|^{1-\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon}\!\leq\!n^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}c_{4}L(n_{0})\big(\tfrac{n_{0}}{n}\big)^{-\varepsilon}\big(\tfrac{n_{0}}{n}\big)^{\frac{1}{\alpha}+\varepsilon}|s^{\prime}\!-\!s|^{1-\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon}\\
\!=\!c_{4}L(n_{0})n_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}|s^{\prime}\!-\!s|^{1-\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon},

which implies (
3.30
) with
c\!=\!c_{2}(\mu,\mathtt{b})(c_{4}L(n_{0})n_{0}^{1/\alpha})^{\mathtt{b}}
.

We next assume that
s^{\prime}-s\!\geq\!\frac{n_{0}}{n}
. It easily implies that
\lfloor ns^{\prime}\rfloor\!-\!\lfloor ns\rfloor\!\geq\!n_{0}
. Since
\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathtt{a}(n)\!=\!\infty
,

\sup_{n\geq n_{0}}\mathtt{a}(n)^{-1}\!=:\!c_{5}\!<\!\infty
. Thus by (
3.36
), it is easy to check the following.

\displaystyle\frac{1+\mathtt{a}(\lfloor ns^{\prime}\rfloor\!-\!\lfloor ns\rfloor)}{\mathtt{a}(n)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1+c_{5})\frac{\mathtt{a}(\lfloor ns^{\prime}\rfloor\!-\!\lfloor ns\rfloor)}{\mathtt{a}(n)}\leq(1+c_{5})c_{4}\big(s^{\prime}\!-\!s+\tfrac{1}{n}\big)^{1-\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{1-\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon}\!(1+c_{5})c_{4}(s^{\prime}\!-\!s)^{1-\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon}.

Thanks to (
3.35
), we get (
3.30
) with
c\!=\!c_{3}(\mu,\mathtt{b})\big(2^{1-\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon}(1\!+\!c_{5})c_{4}\big)^{\mathtt{b}}
. This easily completes the proof of (
3.30
) by taking
c
equal to the largest of the two constants.
Then (
3.31
) is a direct consequence of Lemma
3.11
when
\gamma\!<\!1\!-\!\frac{1}{\alpha}\!-\!\frac{1}{\mathtt{b}}
. When
\gamma\!\in\![1\!-\!\frac{1}{\alpha}\!-\!\frac{1}{\mathtt{b}},1\!-\!\frac{1}{\alpha})
, we use the elementary inequality
x^{\mathtt{b}}\!\leq\!1\!+\!x^{2/(1-\frac{1}{\alpha}-\gamma)}
,
x\geq 0
, to get (
3.31
) anyway. Then, (
3.32
) is an easy consequence from (
3.31
).
\hfill\blacksquare

Proposition 3.18
.

We fix
\alpha\!\in\!(1,2]
and we assume that
\mu
satisfies (
1.3
). Let
\nu
be a supercritical offspring distribution whose mean is denoted by
\mathtt{m}_{\nu}
.
Let
\mathtt{b}_{0}\!\in\!\big(1\!+\!\frac{\alpha}{{\alpha-1}},\infty)
. We assume that
\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}k^{1+2\mathtt{b}_{0}}\nu(k)\!<\!\infty
. Let
\mathtt{b}\!\in\!\big(1\!+\!\tfrac{\alpha}{\alpha-1},\mathtt{b}_{0})
.
Recall from (
2.59
) the definition of
(a_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
.
Let
s_{0}\!\in\!(1,\infty)
and let
\gamma
be a positive real number such that
\gamma\!<\!\tfrac{1}{2}\big(1\!-\!\frac{1}{\alpha})\big(1\!-\!\frac{1}{\mathtt{b}})\!-\!\tfrac{1}{2\mathtt{b}}
(there are such ones since
\mathtt{b}\!>\!1\!+\!\tfrac{\alpha}{\alpha-1}\,
).
Then there exists
c\!\in\!(1,\infty)
that only depends on
\mu,\nu,\mathtt{b},s_{0}
and
\gamma
such that for all
n\!\in\!\mathbb{N}^{*}
and for
s,s^{\prime}\!\in\![0,s_{0}]
,

\textrm{${\bf P}$-a.s.\penalty\ }\quad{\bf E}\Big[\big|\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{a_{n}}}\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{*}_{ns^{\prime}}(\tau_{\infty})\!-\!\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{a_{n}}}\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{*}_{ns}(\tau_{\infty})\big|^{2\mathtt{b}}\big|(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})\Big]\leq c\,K_{\mathtt{b}}(\mathbf{T})\,\big|s^{\prime}\!-\!s\big|^{1+2\mathtt{b}\gamma}\;.

(3.37)

where
K_{\mathtt{b}}(\mathbf{T})
is the
{\bf P}
-a.s. finite and
(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})
-measurable r.v. defined in Proposition
3.4
. Moreover,
conditionally given
(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})
, the laws of the processes

(\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{a_{n}}}\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{*}_{ns}(\tau_{\infty}))_{s\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}
,
n\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
, are tight on
\mathbf{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R})
.

Proof.
To simplify the notation,
we denote by
\mathcal{G}
the
\sigma
-field generated by
(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})
, and by
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
the
\sigma
-field generated by
((\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o}),\tau_{\infty})
. We denote by
C
the contour process
C(\tau_{\infty})
and by
\mathcal{H}
the height process
H(\tau_{\infty})
and we also drop
\tau_{\infty}
in the contour snake
\mathcal{W}
and the height snake
\mathcal{W}^{*}
.
Although the proposition concerns the height snake, it is easier to consider first
the endpoint process of contour snake
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}
conditionally given
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.

Let us fix integers
0\!\leq\!k\!\leq\!l\!\leq\!n_{0}
. We denote by
j
the smallest integer of
[\![k,l]\!]
such that

C_{j}\!=\!\inf_{s\in[k,l]}C_{s}
. By definition of BRWs and by definition of the endpoint process of the contour snake
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}
, conditionally given
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
,
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{k}-\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{j}

(resp.
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{l}-\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{j}
) is distributed as
S_{X_{C_{k}}}\!-\!S_{X_{C_{j}}}
(resp.
S_{X_{C_{l}}}\!-\!S_{X_{C_{j}}}
) where
\overline{X}_{n}\!=\!(\mathbf{T},X_{n})
,
n\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
, stands for a
\mathtt{m}_{\nu}
-biased RW on
\mathbf{T}
starting at
X_{0}\!=\!\mathtt{o}
. Lemma
3.4
applies and we
{\bf P}
-a.s. get

\displaystyle{\bf E}\big[|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{l}\!\!\!\!\!\!

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{k}|^{2\mathtt{b}}\big|\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\big]\leq 2^{2\mathtt{b}-1}\big({\bf E}\big[|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{k}-\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{j}|^{2\mathtt{b}}\big|\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\big]+{\bf E}\big[|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{l}-\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{j}|^{2\mathtt{b}}\big|\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\big]\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{2\mathtt{b}-1}K_{\mathtt{b}}(\mathbf{T})\big(C_{k}\big|C_{k}\!-\!C_{j}\big|^{\mathtt{b}-1}\!+C_{l}\big|C_{l}\!-\!C_{j}\big|^{\mathtt{b}-1}\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{2\mathtt{b}}K_{\mathtt{b}}(\mathbf{T})\,\big(\!\!\!\!\!\max_{\hskip 8.19447ptr\in[0,n_{0}]}\!\!\!\!\!C_{r}\big)\!\!\max_{\;r,r^{\prime}\in[k,l]}\!|C_{r}\!-\!C_{r^{\prime}}|^{\mathtt{b}-1}.

Let
s,s^{\prime}\!\in\![0,n_{0}]
be such that
s^{\prime}\!\geq\!s
. Suppose first that
s,s^{\prime}\!\in\![k,k+1]
. The specific
interpolation of the contour snake implies that
|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{s^{\prime}}\!-\!\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{s}|\!=\!|s^{\prime}\!-\!s|\,|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{k}\!-\!\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{k+1}|
. Thus,

{\bf E}\big[|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{s}\!-\!\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{s^{\prime}}|^{2\mathtt{b}}\big|\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\big]\!\leq\!2^{2\mathtt{b}}K_{\mathtt{b}}(\mathbf{T})\,(\max_{r\in[0,n_{0}]}C_{r})\,|s^{\prime}\!-\!s|^{\mathtt{b}-1}
, since
\max_{\;r,r^{\prime}\in[k,k+1]}|C_{r}\!-\!C_{r^{\prime}}|\!=\!1
and
2\mathtt{b}\!\geq\!\mathtt{b}\!-\!1
.
Now suppose that
s,s^{\prime}\!\in\![0,n_{0}]
are such that
\lceil s\rceil\!\leq\!\lfloor s^{\prime}\rfloor
. Thus,

\displaystyle{\bf E}\Big[|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{s}-\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{s^{\prime}}|^{2\mathtt{b}}\Big|\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\Big]

(3.38)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\!\!\!3^{2\mathtt{b}-1}2^{2\mathtt{b}}K_{\mathtt{b}}(\mathbf{T})\,\big(\!\!\!\!\!\max_{\hskip 8.19447ptr\in[0,n_{0}]}\!\!\!\!\!C_{r}\big)\big(|\lceil s\rceil\!-\!s|^{\mathtt{b}-1}\!\!+|s^{\prime}\!-\!\lfloor s^{\prime}\rfloor|^{\mathtt{b}-1}\!\!+\!\!\!\max_{\;r,r^{\prime}\in[\lceil s\rceil,\lfloor s^{\prime}\rfloor]}\!|C_{r}\!-\!C_{r^{\prime}}|^{\mathtt{b}-1}\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\!\!\!6^{2\mathtt{b}}K_{\mathtt{b}}(\mathbf{T})\,\big(\!\!\!\!\!\max_{\hskip 8.19447ptr\in[0,n_{0}]}\!\!\!\!\!C_{r}\big)\!\!\max_{\;r,r^{\prime}\in[s,s^{\prime}]}\!|C_{r}\!-\!C_{r^{\prime}}|^{\mathtt{b}-1}.

This inequality easily extends to all
s,s^{\prime}\!\in\![0,n_{0}]
such that
s\!\leq\!s^{\prime}
.

Recall that
\mathcal{H}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{*}
are derived from
C
and
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}
by the time-change
\phi_{\tau_{\infty}}\!:\!\mathbb{R}_{+}\!\to\!\mathbb{R}_{+}
that is the increasing continuous bijection defined in Remark
2.5

(\textbf{d})
. Namely,
\mathcal{H}_{s}\!=\!C_{\phi_{\tau_{\infty}}(s)}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{*}_{s}\!=\!\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\phi_{\tau_{\infty}}(s)}
,
s\!\in\!\mathbb{R}_{+}
. Then
for all
s_{1},s^{\prime},s\!\in\!\mathbb{R}_{+}
such that
s_{1}\!\geq\!s^{\prime}\!\geq\!s
, by choosing
n_{0}\!=\!\phi_{\tau_{\infty}}(\lceil s_{1}\rceil)
in (
3.38
), we
{\bf P}
-a.s. get

{\bf E}\Big[|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{*}_{s}\!-\!\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{*}_{s^{\prime}}|^{2\mathtt{b}}\Big|\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\Big]\leq 6^{2\mathtt{b}}K_{\mathtt{b}}(\mathbf{T})\!\!\!\max_{\>r\in[0,\lceil s_{1}\rceil]}\!\!\!\!\!\mathcal{H}_{r}\max_{\;r,r^{\prime}\in[s,s^{\prime}]}\!|\mathcal{H}_{r}\!-\!\mathcal{H}_{r^{\prime}}|^{\mathtt{b}-1}.

(3.39)

We next fix
s_{0}\!\in\!(0,\infty)
and
0\!<\!\gamma\!<\!\tfrac{1}{2}\big(1\!-\!\frac{1}{\alpha})\big(1\!-\!\frac{1}{\mathtt{b}})\!-\!\tfrac{1}{2\mathtt{b}}
. Note that
\gamma_{0}\!:=\!\frac{1+2\mathtt{b}\gamma}{\mathtt{b}-1}\!<\!1\!-\!\frac{1}{\alpha}
. We set

Z_{n}\!=\!\mathtt{Hol}_{\gamma_{0},s_{0}}(\tfrac{1}{a_{n}}\mathcal{H}_{n\,\cdot})
and
\Gamma_{\!n}\!=\!\max_{r\in[0,\lceil s_{0}\rceil]}\tfrac{1}{a_{n}}\mathcal{H}_{nr}
. Then (
3.39
) entails

{\bf E}\Big[|\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{a_{n}}}\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{*}_{ns}\!-\!\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{a_{n}}}\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{*}_{ns^{\prime}}|^{2\mathtt{b}}\Big|\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\Big]\leq 6^{2\mathtt{b}}K_{\mathtt{b}}(\mathbf{T})\,\Gamma_{\!n}Z_{n}^{\mathtt{b}-1}|s\!-\!s^{\prime}|^{1+2\mathtt{b}\gamma}.

Since
\gamma_{0}\!<\!1\!-\!\frac{1}{\alpha}
, Proposition
3.17
applies: there is
c_{1}\!\in\!(0,\infty)
, which
only depends on
\mathtt{b}
,
\mu
,
s_{0}
and
\gamma_{0}
such that
\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}({\bf E}[\Gamma_{\!n}^{\mathtt{b}}]+{\bf E}[Z_{n}^{\mathtt{b}}])\leq c_{1}
, which implies
\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}{\bf E}[\Gamma_{\!n}Z_{n}^{\mathtt{b}-1}]\!\leq\!c_{1}
by Hölder inequality. So we obtain

{\bf E}\Big[|\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{a_{n}}}\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{*}_{ns}\!-\!\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{a_{n}}}\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{*}_{ns^{\prime}}|^{2\mathtt{b}}\Big|\mathcal{G}\Big]\leq 6^{2\mathtt{b}}K_{\mathtt{b}}(\mathbf{T})\,{\bf E}\big[\Gamma_{\!n}Z^{\mathtt{b}-1}_{n}\big]\,|s\!-\!s^{\prime}|^{1+2\mathtt{b}\gamma}\leq 6^{2\mathtt{b}}c_{1}K_{\mathtt{b}}(\mathbf{T})\,|s\!-\!s^{\prime}|^{1+2\mathtt{b}\gamma},

since
\tau_{\infty}
is independent of
(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})
. This proves (
3.37
). Conditional tightness is a consequence of Lemmas
3.11
and
3.12
.
\hfill\blacksquare

3.4
Proof of Proposition
3.10

We keep the notation and the general assumptions discussed in Section
3.2
.
In particular, we recall (
3.13
):
\mathscr{Q}_{n}\!\to\!(\mathbf{g}_{1},\mathbf{d}_{1},X,H,H)
weakly on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\!\times\!\mathbf{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R})\!\times\!\mathbf{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R})^{2}
.
By Skorokhod representation theorem,
there are processes
\mathscr{Q}_{n}^{\prime}
with the same law as
\mathscr{Q}_{n}
that converge almost surely to
(\mathbf{g}^{\prime}_{1},\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{1},X^{\prime},H^{\prime},H^{\prime})
, which has the same law as
(\mathbf{g}_{1},\mathbf{d}_{1},X,H,H)
.
Without loss of generality, we can assume that
(\Omega,\mathscr{F},{\bf P})
is large enough to allow the almost sure convergence to take place.
Then observe that
(\mathbf{g}^{\prime}_{1},\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{1})
is necessarily the first excursion interval of
H^{\prime}
longer than
1
, and

\mathbf{g}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{1}
are thus derived from
H^{\prime}
as in Remark
2.28
.
Since the Lukasiewicz path (or the height process) of a forest completely encodes it, there actually exists a sequence of GW(
\mu
)-forests
\tau_{n,\infty}
,
n\!\in\!\mathbb{N}^{*}
, independent from the environment
(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})
and such that

\mathscr{Q}_{n}^{\prime}=\big(\tfrac{1}{n}\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}_{n},\tfrac{1}{n}\mathfrak{d}^{\prime}_{n},\tfrac{1}{b_{n}}V_{\lfloor n\cdot\rfloor}(\tau_{n,\infty}),\tfrac{1}{a_{n}}H_{n\cdot}(\tau_{n,\infty}),\tfrac{1}{a_{n}}C_{2n\cdot}(\tau_{n,\infty})\big)

(3.40)

where
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}_{n}
and
\mathfrak{d}^{\prime}_{n}
are derived from
\tau_{n,\infty}
as
\mathfrak{g}_{n}
and
\mathfrak{d}_{n}
are from
\tau_{\infty}
in (
2.58
) (roughly speaking
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}_{n}
and
\mathfrak{d}^{\prime}_{n}
are the endpoints of the first excursion of
H_{n\cdot}(\tau_{n,\infty})
longer than
n
). Namely we assume that

\textrm{${\bf P}$-a.s.}\qquad\mathscr{Q}_{n}^{\prime}\underset{n\to\infty}{-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow}(\mathbf{g}^{\prime}_{1},\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{1},X^{\prime},H^{\prime},H^{\prime})

(3.41)

in
\mathbb{R}^{2}\!\times\!\mathbf{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R})\!\times\!\mathbf{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R})^{2}
.

Then, for all
n\!\in\!\mathbb{N}^{*}
,
we introduce the process
(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{*}_{s}(\tau_{n,\infty}))_{s\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}
as a
\mathbb{R}
-valued process such that

\Big(\tau_{n,\infty},\big(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{*}_{s}(\tau_{n,\infty})\big)_{s\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}\Big)\overset{\textrm{(law)}}{=}\Big(\tau_{\infty},\big(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{*}_{s}(\tau_{\infty})\big)_{s\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}\Big),

(3.42)

where
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{*}(\tau_{\infty})
is defined as previously.
This section is mainly devoted to the proof of Proposition
3.19
below. Before proving it, we first show that it actually implies
Proposition
3.10
.

Proposition 3.19
.

We fix
\alpha\!\in\!(1,2]
. Let
\mu
satisfy (
1.3
). Let
\nu
be a supercritical offspring distribution whose mean is denoted by
\mathtt{m}_{\nu}
. We assume that there exists
\mathtt{b}\!\in\!(2,\infty)
such that

\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}k^{\mathtt{b}}\nu(k)\!<\!\infty
. Let
(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})
be an invariant GW(
\nu
)-tree.
Let
\tau_{n,\infty}
,
n\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
, be GW(
\mu
)-forests that are independent from
(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})
and
such that (
3.41
) holds true. Let
(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{*}_{s}(\tau_{n,\infty}))_{s\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}
be as in (
3.42
). We denote by
(W^{\prime}_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}
a one-dimensional Brownian snake starting at
0
and whose lifetime process is
H^{\prime}
.
We recall from (
3.6
) the definition of
\sigma_{\nu}
. Then for all
p\!\in\!\mathbb{N}^{*}
and for all real numbers
0\!\leq\!s_{1}\!<\!\ldots\!<\!s_{p}
and
\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{p}
,
{\bf P}
-almost surely

\lim_{n\to\infty}{\bf E}\Big[\prod_{1\leq j\leq p}\exp\Big(i\tfrac{1}{\sigma_{\nu}\sqrt{a_{n}}}\lambda_{j}\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{*}_{\!\lfloor ns_{j}\rfloor}(\tau_{n,\infty})\Big)\,\Big|\,(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o}),\tau_{n,\infty}\Big]={\bf E}\Big[\prod_{1\leq j\leq p}e^{i\lambda_{j}\widehat{W}^{\prime}_{s_{j}}}\,\Big|H^{\prime}\Big].

(3.43)

Proof.
The proof of Proposition
3.19
starts below, after the proof of Proposition
3.10
.
\hfill\blacksquare

Proof of Proposition
3.10
.
As already explained, the time-change (
3.21
) and (
3.22
) combined with Lemma
3.13
implies that it sufficient to prove conditionally given
(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})
that the following convergence

\Big(\tfrac{1}{n}\mathfrak{g}_{n},\tfrac{1}{n}\mathfrak{d}_{n},\tfrac{1}{b_{n}}V_{\lfloor n\cdot\rfloor}(\tau_{\infty}),\tfrac{1}{a_{n}}H_{n\cdot}(\tau_{\infty}),\tfrac{1}{\sigma_{\nu}\sqrt{a_{n}}}\,\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{*}_{n\cdot}(\tau_{\infty})\Big)\underset{n\to\infty}{-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow}\big(\mathbf{g}_{1},\mathbf{d}_{1},X,H,\widehat{W}\big)

(3.44)

holds weakly on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\!\times\!\mathbf{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R})\!\times\!\mathbf{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R})^{2}
equipped with the product topology.

We recall (
3.41
) and we now prove that Proposition
3.19
implies Proposition
3.10
.
First recall for all
s\!>\!0
that
{\bf P}
-a.s.
X^{\prime}_{s-}\!=\!X^{\prime}_{s}
. By standard results on Skorokhod topology (see Lemma
3.20

(ii)
below), it
{\bf P}
-a.s. implies that
\lim_{n\to\infty}\tfrac{1}{b_{n}}V_{\lfloor ns\rfloor}(\tau_{n,\infty})\!=\!X^{\prime}_{s}
.
By Proposition
3.19
and dominated convergence, we then get
for all real numbers
0\!\leq\!s_{1}\!<\!\ldots\!<\!s_{p}
and

\xi,\xi^{\prime},\lambda_{1},\lambda^{\prime}_{1},\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{p},\lambda^{\prime}_{p},\lambda_{p}^{\prime\prime}
,

\displaystyle\textrm{${\bf P}$-a.s.\penalty\ }\quad\lim_{n\to\infty}{\bf E}\Big[e^{i\tfrac{1}{n}\xi\mathfrak{g}_{n}+i\tfrac{1}{n}\xi^{\prime}\mathfrak{d}_{n}}\!\!\prod_{1\leq j\leq p}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!e^{i\tfrac{1}{b_{n}}\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{j}V_{\lfloor ns_{j}\rfloor}(\tau_{\infty})+i\tfrac{1}{a_{n}}\lambda^{\prime}_{j}H_{ns_{j}}(\tau_{\infty})+i\tfrac{1}{\sigma_{\nu}\sqrt{a_{n}}}\lambda_{j}\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{*}_{\!ns_{j}}(\tau_{\infty})}\,\Big|\,(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})\Big]

(3.45)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\bf E}\Big[e^{i\xi\mathbf{g}_{1}+i\xi^{\prime}\mathbf{d}_{1}}\!\!\prod_{1\leq j\leq p}e^{i\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{j}X_{s_{j}}+i\lambda^{\prime}_{j}H_{s_{j}}+i\lambda_{j}\widehat{W}_{s_{j}}}\Big].

To simplify notation, we denote by
\mathcal{G}
the
\sigma
-field generated by
(\mathbf{T},\mathtt{o})
. We also denote by
\mathbf{D}_{\!q}
the space
\mathbf{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}^{q})
of cadlag functions equipped with the Skorokhod topology, and we also recall the shorthand notation
\mathbf{C}^{0}_{1}
for
\mathbf{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R})
which is equipped with the topology of uniform convergence on every compact interval. Below, product spaces are equipped with product topology and if
E
is a Polish space, we denote by
\mathcal{M}_{1}(E)
the space of its Borel probability measures equipped with the weak convergence.
The following elementary
lemma sets the notation and the basic results we need.

Lemma 3.20
.

The following properties hold true.

(i)

The function
\jmath:(\mathtt{u},\mathtt{x},\mathtt{h},\mathtt{w})\!\in\!\mathbb{R}^{2}\!\times\!\mathbf{D}_{1}\!\times\!(\mathbf{C}^{0}_{1})^{2}\!\!\mapsto\!\big(\mathtt{u},(\mathtt{x}(s),\mathtt{h}(s),\mathtt{w}(s))_{s\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big)\!\in\!\mathbb{R}^{2}\!\times\!\mathbf{D}_{3}
is continuous.

(ii)

Let
s_{1},\ldots,s_{p}\!\in\!\mathbb{R}_{+}
. The function
\phi_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{p}}:\mathtt{z}\!\in\!\mathbf{D}_{3}\mapsto(\mathtt{z}(s_{1}),\ldots,\mathtt{z}(s_{p}))\!\in\!(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{p}
is continuous at
\mathtt{z}\!\in\!\mathbf{D}_{3}
such that
\mathtt{z}(s_{j}-)\!=\!\mathtt{z}(s_{j})
,
1\!\leq\!j\!\leq\!p
.

(iii)

Let
k\!\in\!\{1,2,3\}
. The function
